# Blackbird portfolio & partner reference

Living reference for **The Blackbird Brief** (`podcasts/blackbird-brief/`).
Defines what "portfolio-relevant" means and lists the people, targets,
institutions, and competitors to track. **Keep current** — update when a
program advances, a competitor moves, or a new spinout/lead appears.

Previously verified 2026-08-23 (Sunday two-episode day — Sourcing Radar + Portfolio Watch). **SOURCING RADAR LEAD = THE BRAKE ON COCHLEAR HAIR-CELL REGENERATION HAS A NAME, IT IS STILL BEING ACTIVELY APPLIED, AND THE NODE UNDERNEATH IT IS DRUGGABLE — AND THE LIABILITY IT CREATES IS EXACTLY WHAT SELEXON EXISTS TO SOLVE.** Morgan CT, Rehman ZU, **Doetzlhofer A** (senior), "Loss of Zbtb20 disrupts cochlear supporting cell differentiation and maturation and extends the postnatal hair cell regenerative window in mice," bioRxiv **10.64898/2026.08.19.745776v1**, posted **2026-08-20**. Solomon H. Snyder Dept of Neuroscience + Dept of Otolaryngology–Head & Neck Surgery / Center for Hearing and Balance, **JHU SOM**. **FULL TEXT OBTAINED VIA A ROUTE WORTH REMEMBERING: the jina reader on the bioRxiv `.full` HTML returned abstract-only (26KB, not yet rendered), but `r.jina.ai` on the `v1.full.pdf` returned the complete 35-page body (75KB). When a fresh preprint is un-rendered, TRY THE PDF THROUGH JINA BEFORE DEFERRING.** **FINDINGS.** Emx2-Cre Zbtb20 KO delays cell-cycle exit, differentiation and maturation of cochlear supporting cells (SCs); progenitor genes **Hmga2** and **Sox11** fail to switch off by P5 (676 DEGs, 489 up / 187 down, q<0.05; downregulated include Gdf6, Aqp4, **Gjb6**). Purified **Lfng-GFP+** KO SCs form organoids at **~3× the control rate**, larger, with more EdU+ dividing JAG1+ cells, and produce significantly more MYO7A+/SOX2+ hair cells per organoid. In **Pou4f3-DTR** explants, KO SCs produce more hair cells in BOTH damaged and undamaged tissue. **THE COMMERCIALLY LOAD-BEARING RESULT — ACUTE deletion (R26-rtTA;TetO-Cre + dox) in already-formed postnatal tissue STILL increases organoid size and formation rate → the repression is MAINTAINED, not a one-time developmental setting.** Acute-KO RNA-seq (524 DEGs) → upregulation of **Ptprz1**, receptor for **midkine (MDK)**; exogenous MDK at 10/50/100 ng/mL reproduces the phenotype (size up at all doses; formation rate up at 10 and 50 but NOT 100 = dose ceiling). Acute deletion in explants produced hair cells only in **damaged** tissue (mid-turn). **IP POSTURE = CLEAN.** "The authors declare no competing interests." Funding **NIDCD R01DC019359 (A.D.) + F31DC020882 (C.M.)** and the **David M. Rubenstein Fund for Hearing Research**. No pharma sponsor, no company, no equity. GEO **GSE342694**. Zbtb20-floxed mice from **Ulrich Mueller (JHU SOM)**. **WHY IT IS A BLACKBIRD LEAD AND NOT SOMEBODY ELSE'S PAPER:** the therapeutic concept (turn up MDK–PTPRZ1 in cochlear SCs) carries a targeting liability — MDK/PTPRZ1 is a proliferation axis with **oncology history (PTPRZ1 is a glioma target)** — so it must be restricted to supporting cells, at a controlled level, in a small semi-enclosed compartment. **That is Aletira's premise, and supporting cells are already the target cell type for the GJB2 programs. Framing for Avi + Hemaka: not "build a midkine company" but "is this a SECOND PAYLOAD for SELEXON, delivered locally into the cochlea, where expression restriction is the SAFETY story rather than a nice-to-have."** **COMPETITIVE PRECEDENT (state it before an investor does): Frequency Therapeutics ran this exact thesis** — small molecules (GSK3 + HDAC inhibition) to wake Lgr5+ cochlear SCs — **and FX-322 missed speech perception and every secondary in a 142-patient Phase 2b (Feb 2023); the company discontinued FX-322 and FX-345, cut ~55% of staff, and pivoted to remyelination in MS.** The difference: Frequency pushed generic stemness pathways without knowing what enforced the mature state; this paper names the enforcer and hands over a specific downstream ligand–receptor axis. **Pitch it as "the field pushed the wrong node," not "hair-cell regeneration, take four."** **CANDID LIMITS:** organoid + explant only; no in vivo regeneration in a mature animal; **no ABR / functional hearing recovery anywhere in the paper**; all P5 mouse tissue (a permissive window that does not exist in an adult human cochlea); acute deletion needed damage as a partner. **DILIGENCE ASK (narrow and cheap, and it IS the investment case): does MDK–PTPRZ1 activation increase hair-cell counts in an ADULT mouse cochlea in vivo after noise or ototoxic damage, with an ABR readout?** **ACTIONS: Esther — open the conversation this week. Eddie — provisional-filing moment on the MDK–PTPRZ1 axis specifically, NOT on the transcription factor.** **RADAR SECOND ITEM = WATCH, NOT A BUILD.** Opendak M (senior, **Kennedy Krieger**) + Haughey N (JHU, metabolomics), "Bile acid signaling as a therapeutically tractable pathway linking early caregiving adversity to social behavior," bioRxiv 10.64898/2026.08.14.742173v1, posted 2026-08-19 (full text read). Social — not non-social — adversity reorganises peripheral bile acid + tryptophan metabolism and BLA co-expression networks; **oral chenodeoxycholic acid (CDCA), but NOT cholic acid, rescues the infant affiliative deficit**, attributed to CDCA being the most potent endogenous **FXR** agonist. No competing interests; NIH R00MH124434/R01MH133456 (MO), R01MH131469/R01MH131219 (NH), Kennedy Krieger IDDRC P50HD105328/U54HD079123. **Why watch: the rescuing compound is a decades-old GENERIC (chenodiol) — unownable; the ownable pieces are a method of use, a proprietary FXR agonist (crowded, bruised field with pruritus/lipid liabilities), or the serum bile-acid panel as a stratification biomarker. Endpoint is infant rat social approach; effect sex-specific and the responding sex FLIPPED between the two models. Esther: keep Opendak on the watch list.** **RADAR DEFERRAL (retry first next week): Taylor JE, Sharma P, Krantz B, "Single-Molecule Proteomics via a Dynamic Translocase and Physics-Informed Machine Learning," bioRxiv 10.64898/2026.08.17.745284v1, UMB** — anthrax toxin protective antigen as a DYNAMIC nanopore (time-domain thermodynamic fingerprinting vs static excluded-volume), physics-informed ML, resolves Leu/Ile, **98.02±0.05% accuracy on five untagged native clinical biomarker peptides including KRAS G12D**, low-nanomolar. Body not rendered; **the jina→PDF trick 429'd on retry — try again next run.** Real platform potential in the Quantum-Si / Nautilus / Glyphic lane. **RADAR SWEPT AND PASSED:** JHU leptomeningeal blood–CSF barrier atlas (10.64898/2026.08.19.745529v1, descriptive, no target); UMB Baskakov astrocytic **Stat3** KO in prion disease (10.64898/2026.08.15.745015v1 — no survival benefit in SSLOW, modest male-only delay in 22L = honest negative); UMD Hao lab stem-cell secretome proteomics methods paper (10.64898/2026.08.18.745538v1); JHU Coppens ΔDGAT1 live-attenuated Toxoplasma vaccine (10.64898/2026.08.16.745102v1, niche). **Lieber Institute: ZERO corresponding-author preprints in the window.** Sweep = 1,576 bioRxiv + 702 medRxiv records 08-17→08-23, day-by-day via api.biorxiv.org details (note: biorxiv pages 30 at a time, medrxiv 100 — the loop must page on `total`, not on a fixed page size). **PORTFOLIO WATCH LEAD (event 2026-08-19; lands on the Lieber GPR52 NewCo and on the UM Ventures surface): KYNEXIS closed a €40M Series A EXTENSION → €97M total (~$110M), LED BY THE NOVARTIS VENTURE FUND** (new investor; **Marianne Uteng** to the board, **Mathias Frederiksen** observer), with Forbion, Ysios and Sunstone following. Alzheimer's.** **THREE READS. (1) COMPETITIVE CLOCK ON GPR52 JUST SHORTENED.** The GPR52 value case is all-three-symptom-domains, and **cognition carries the value**. MapLight (08-10) was a cognition signal in a *subgroup* of a psychosis-powered trial; **Kynexis is a trial designed around cognition, in a company built entirely on that claim, now with large-pharma venture money and stated registrational prep.** A dedicated, purpose-built, non-dopaminergic CIAS readout enters the public record **before our DC declaration (expected later 2026)**. **Positive → first-in-class in CIAS is gone and our differentiation must be BREADTH + tolerability, not novelty. Previously verified 2026-08-22 (Saturday — Portfolio Watch only). **LEAD = A CHRONIC-PAIN MOLECULE THAT FAILED A U.S. PHASE 3 FOR FUTILITY IS NOW WORTH $500M, AND THE ONLY THING THAT CHANGED IS THE ENTRY DOOR.** 2026-08-21: **WEREWOLF THERAPEUTICS (Nasdaq: HOWL) + AMBROS THERAPEUTICS** all-stock merger + concurrent **OVERSUBSCRIBED $150M private placement co-led by RA CAPITAL MANAGEMENT and JANUS HENDERSON**. Combined co. = **Ambros Therapeutics, San Diego, ticker AMBX**; **Ambros valued $500M / Werewolf $47.5M**; ownership **~71.7% Ambros / ~21.5% new investors / ~6.8% legacy Werewolf**; funded through **2028 topline and into 1H 2029**; **CEO Jay Hagan** (Ambros team: CFO Cris Calsada, CMO Gail Cawkwell, GC Christopher Aker, SVP CorpDev Kunal Kishnani); close expected **by Q1 2027** subject to both stockholder votes. On top of a **$125M Series A closed 2025-12-16** (co-led **RA Capital + Enavate Sciences/Patient Square**, with **Abiogen Pharma**, Janus Henderson, **ARKIN BIO — note: ALSO SKYLARK BIO'S INVESTOR**, Balyasny, Transhuman, Adage) = **~$275M in eight months**. **ASSET = NERIDRONATE (AMBTX-01)**, an amino-bisphosphonate (FDPS inhibitor, bone resorption) **licensed from Abiogen Pharma**; **approved in Italy for CRPS-1 since 2014** (Nerixia; also osteogenesis imperfecta + Paget's), **~600,000 patients across its Italian indications**. CRPS-1 = orphan pain disorder, **~65,000 new U.S. cases/yr, NO FDA-approved treatment**; **Breakthrough Therapy + Fast Track + Orphan Drug** all in hand. **THE ANALYTICAL CORE (original work — both registry protocols pulled and compared 2026-08-22): GRÜNENTHAL ALREADY FAILED WITH THIS MOLECULE.** Its two Ph3s — **KF7013-02 / NCT03530345 (n=182)** and **KF7013-04 / NCT03560986 (n=267)** — were **TERMINATED in 2019 after a PRE-PLANNED POOLED INTERIM FUTILITY ANALYSIS**; futility criterion = observed neridronic acid vs placebo mean difference **≥ −0.3 points on the 11-point NRS**; independent statistician recommended stopping. (Also NCT02402530, Ph2/3 n=459, completed 2016; NCT02972359 open-label safety n=580.) **Ambros CRPS-RISE (NCT07210515, n=270, 24 U.S. sites, RECRUITING since 2026-04-14, primary completion 2027-12, topline 2028) KEEPS EVERYTHING THAT FAILED: SAME molecule, SAME 400 mg cumulative IV dose across Days 1/4/7/10, SAME primary endpoint (change from baseline in average pain intensity, 11-pt NRS, Week 12).** **WHAT CHANGED IS ENTIRELY THE ENTRY DOOR.** Grünenthal: CRPS broadly per Budapest criteria, **duration up to TWO YEARS** since onset, **no subtype restriction, no imaging requirement**, and **prior failure of ≥2 treatments REQUIRED** (chronic/refractory/heterogeneous), 71 sites / 8 countries. Ambros: **CRPS-1 only**, single limb, no known peripheral nerve injury, **≤6 MONTHS since onset**, **WARM SUBTYPE ONLY** (edema + ≥2 of obvious redness / ≥1°C temperature rise / moderate-to-severe edema), **POSITIVE TRIPLE-PHASE BONE SCAN with CENTRAL READ**, **CRPS-2 and CRPS-NOS EXCLUDED**, cases with no identified inciting event excluded, **PAIN CATASTROPHIZING SCALE ≥40 EXCLUDED**, **eDiary-compliance gate during screening**. **= FIVE ENRICHMENT GATES on an unchanged drug at an unchanged dose against an unchanged endpoint — and a syndicate that knew about the first failure paid $500M for the second attempt.** **THREE READS.** **(1) GUT GCPII PROGRAM (Hemaka + Barbara Slusher, JHU Drug Discovery) — THE DURABLE LESSON.** Since the 08-14 Fibrx transaction the standing line has been that inflammation and fibrosis in Crohn's are spoken for and **PAIN IS THE LAST UNSTAKED AXIS**. This deal **prices what staking it costs, and the currency is NOT mechanism** — nobody paid for novelty (decades-old bisphosphonate); **they paid for a STRATIFICATION PACKAGE**. **Reframed program question: not 'does gut-restricted GCPII inhibition relieve visceral pain' but 'WHICH IBD PATIENTS, IDENTIFIED BY WHAT OBJECTIVE MEASURE, BEFORE DOSING.'** If an enrichable subpopulation exists (visceral-hypersensitivity phenotype, imaging/biomarker readout, disease-duration window), **that is the registration path and it belongs in the program plan at the PRECLINICAL stage**, not discovered after a Ph2 misses on all-comers. **CANDID LIMITS (state them):** neridronate acts on bone resorption, nothing to do with the enteric nervous system; and an orphan indication with **no** approved therapy is a far cleaner regulatory setup than pain in Crohn's, where every patient is already on an approved anti-inflammatory and you must show benefit on top. **What transfers is TRIAL ARCHITECTURE + FINANCING, not biology.** **(2) EDDIE — THE REVERSE-MERGER TEMPLATE IS NOW PRICED.** Second Nasdaq-shell reverse merger in **EIGHT DAYS** (after Skye+Redx → Fibrx, 08-14), in a year with **~18 of them, most since 2018**. Terms now visible: **shell = $47.5M, legacy holders = 6.8%**. For a Phase-2-ready Baltimore asset that does not want a conventional offering, that arithmetic is on the record twice in one week. **(3) CLASP — THE UNCOMFORTABLE HALF.** Werewolf was a **PLATFORM** company: conditionally activated **IL-2 (WTX-124)** and **IL-12 (WTX-330) INDUKINE** molecules = precision delivery in oncology, a first cousin to how several portfolio assets describe themselves. Opened a strategic review **2026-02-24** (Piper Sandler), could not get a clinical asset over the line, ended as a **listing vehicle worth 6.8%** of the company that took its ticker. **Not alarming, CLARIFYING: the platform narrative is not what gets funded right now — the GUARDIAN-101 monotherapy readout is.** **SECOND ITEM — CALENDAR (Aletira regulatory-climate thread): 2026-08-22 IS CAPRICOR'S PDUFA TARGET for deramiocel (DMD).** 07-29 adcomm voted **9–3 against**, but on **CARDIAC FUNCTION (a SECONDARY)**, not on **upper-limb function (the PRIMARY the trial hit)**; company is amending the BLA with **24-mo HOPE-3 extension data**, narrowing to the endpoint it hit — **which may extend the date**. **This is the live test of the 08-18 framing (agency RIGOROUS ON EVIDENCE, FLEXIBLE ON PATH; rewards NARROWING)**, the frame sitting under the SELEXON expression-control pitch. Ultragenyx printed 4 days early this week → **a Saturday action date does not mean nothing happens; watch 08-22 and Mon 08-24.** **BURN NOTE (Matt + Emily): 2026-08-21 KAOS CAPITAL went public** demanding an immediate meeting, **two independent director nominations**, a board-led **M&A / Strategic Alternatives Committee** chaired by a shareholder-backed director, and a **capital-preservation plan** — **the day before the action date**. Capricor cash **$318.1M (YE2025) → $237.9M (2026-06-30) = $80.2M in six months while awaiting a decision on a filed BLA.** **THIRD — GENE-THERAPY CLIMATE (2026-08-21): Reps. JOHN MOOLENAAR (R-MI) + BEN CLINE (R-VA)** formally asked FDA to **reject Chinese clinical data unless trial sites have had a recent FDA audit**, and to **review products already approved on Chinese data**. Previously verified 2026-08-20 (Thursday — Portfolio Watch only). **LEAD = THE FDA APPROVED A GENE THERAPY WHOSE TRANSGENE IS REGULATED BY THE GENE'S OWN NATIVE PROMOTER. Ultragenyx GENGLYCOS (pariglasgene brecaparvovec-opnr, ex-DTX401) — ACCELERATED APPROVAL 2026-08-19, four days AHEAD of the 08-23 (Sunday) PDUFA (the 08-19 episode guided Fri 08-21 / Mon 08-24 — it printed Wed 08-19; note the miss).** Indication: reduce daily cornstarch intake as an adjunct to nutritional management in GSDIa, **ages 8+**. Single IV infusion; **AAV8**; **G6Pase under the NATIVE G6PC promoter**, chosen so treated hepatocytes still respond to **insulin and cortisol** (physiological/dynamic regulation, not merely cell-type restriction). Basis: 48-week randomized double-blind placebo-controlled Ph3 **GlucoGene**, n=46 dosed (mITT n=44; DTX401 n=20 vs placebo n=24) at **1.0e13 GC/kg**, reduction in cornstarch requirement **p<0.001**; crossover at Wk48, analyses at Wk96/Wk144. **ACCELERATED approval on a BURDEN-OF-CARE SURROGATE (cornstarch grams)** — continued approval contingent on confirmatory verification. PMR: 2 yrs safety+efficacy from open-label commercial treatment of **50 patients + 20 controls**, via the existing GSDIa Disease Monitoring Program, 10-yr total follow-up. Manufactured at Ultragenyx's own GTMF, Bedford MA. **FOUR ALETIRA READS, and they do NOT all point the same way — this is the episode's spine:** **(1) TAILWIND / THE CITATION.** Approved AAV products ALREADY use restricted promoters (Hemgenix LP1 + Roctavian HLP in liver; Elevidys MHCK7 in muscle) — so "first tissue-restricted promoter" would be WRONG. What is genuinely new is **NATIVE-promoter PHYSIOLOGICAL regulation** rather than a synthetic tissue-specific promoter. Stack against the 08-19 finding: **Skylark restricts at the 3'UTR; Ultragenyx restricts at the promoter; Aletira restricts at the SPLICE — three companies, three elements on the SAME molecule, one shared premise (the gene's endogenous regulatory grammar should govern the transgene), and that premise now has an FDA APPROVAL behind it.** Best external endorsement SELEXON's thesis has ever had; specific enough to quote. **Action: Geoff Lynn should be using it within the week.** **(2) THE CORRECTION — put it on the record BEFORE an investor does. Native-promoter control did NOT make the product safe.** Label: **ALT/AST elevation 71%**; **corticosteroids administered to ALL patients** post-infusion as standard mitigation; **adrenal insufficiency 24%** (incl. serious events, from the mitigation, not the transgene); **Cushingoid features 14%**; **anaphylaxis 10%**; nausea 38%, hypertriglyceridemia 29%, headache 24%; 7 SAEs in the 48-wk PEAP (anaphylaxis/IR ×2, adrenal insufficiency ×2, high lactate ×2, hypoglycemia ×1); **CONTRAINDICATED in severe hepatic fibrosis or cirrhosis**; vector-integration/theoretical-tumorigenicity language; 12-mo contraception for women, 6-mo semen-donation ban; 3-mo shedding precautions. **DURABLE LINE: EXPRESSION CONTROL IS NOT AN IMMUNOLOGY ANSWER. The immune problem in systemic AAV is the CAPSID AND THE DOSE, not the cassette. Pitching SELEXON as a general "safety platform" is now FALSIFIABLE IN PUBLIC; the defensible claim is narrower and stronger — SELEXON solves OFF-TARGET EXPRESSION, which is the problem that decides whether the therapeutic works in the cell you meant. Action (Hugh Wells + Avi): tighten that sentence.** **(3) BEACHHEAD ARGUMENT (small, elegant, ours to use without naming Skylark): the agreed PMR CONTROL GROUP = "patients who sought commercial treatment but cannot be treated with GENGLYCOS due to the presence of ANTI-AAV8 ANTIBODIES."** Pre-existing seroprevalence exclusion is now reliable enough that FDA lets it constitute a comparator arm — a **systemic-delivery tax** that a **local micro-dose into a small semi-enclosed compartment (the ear) largely avoids**. **(4) MONEY + CLOCK — NEW STRATEGIC ITEM FOR MATT + EDDIE. Becerra*.) **2026 PRV sale prices: Rocket $180M (Jun), Denali $195M (Jul 27), Immedica $200M (May 18), Jazz $200M (JPM26/Jan).** **ARITHMETIC: Aletira is PRECLINICAL — a wholly owned first-in-human program started from here cannot reach approval before 2029-09-30. Previously verified 2026-08-19 (Wednesday — Portfolio Watch only). **LEAD = ORIGINAL WORK: the 08-18 ACTION ITEM IS CLOSED. THREE FINDINGS + one investor gap filled.** **(1) THE MECHANISM — IT IS NOT SPLICING, BUT IT IS NEARER THAN EXPECTED.** Skylark has STILL not disclosed it in words: all company language (incl. the first-patient boilerplate and the ASGCT-2026 About paragraph) says only "a proprietary capsid engineered for BROAD TRANSDUCTION" enabling "targeted, MICRO-DOSE delivery with CELL-SPECIFIC EXPRESSION CONTROL" plus "durable benefit in non-dividing cells of interest." But there is exactly **ONE published Skylark application in the world: WO2025179016A1, "Recombinant adeno associated virus and uses thereof," priority 2024-02-20, filed 2025-02-20, published 2025-08-28**, assignee Skylark Bio, Inc.; inventors **May Kwang-Mei Wang, William J. Neidermyer Jr., Will J. McLean**. Claimed invention = an **ENGINEERED 3' UNTRANSLATED REGION comprising "two or more non-identical nucleotide sequences" derived from the target gene's OWN NATIVE 3'UTR**, to "promote tissue- and cell type-specific expression" and restrict AAV-mediated expression to the cells with endogenous expression of that gene. **CRITICAL NUANCE: the promoters recited in the filing are the ORDINARY UBIQUITOUS ones (CBA, CMV, CAG, SV40, EF1α) — so Skylark is NOT restricting at the transcriptional door at all; it restricts POST-transcriptionally, at the OTHER END of the same molecule, using the native gene's own regulatory grammar.** So the 08-18 dichotomy ("promoter → we're differentiated; splicing → emergency") was the WRONG pair: the real answer is a THIRD option, 3'UTR-based post-transcriptional restriction. **SELEXON's layer (alternative splicing) is UNTAKEN — Geoff Lynn can say that and it holds up — but the nearest IP neighbour is on the same molecule one element over, not a promoter one layer up. ACTION UPGRADED from nice-to-know to a REAL FREEDOM-TO-OPERATE OPINION from counsel BEFORE the raise, specifically on the 3'UTR claims and how broadly they are drafted.** **WRINKLE that sets the follow-up: WO2025179016A1 covers the PENDRIN program (SLC26A4 — Pendred syndrome, DFNB4, enlarged vestibular aqueduct), NOT GJB2.** Named target cells: root cells, spindle cells, outer sulcus cells, epithelial cells of spiral prominence, endolymphatic sac cells, vestibular cells. Capsids recited: AAV1/2/5/7/8/9, AAV-PHP.B, **AAV-S (preferred)**. We have seen the METHOD, not the LEAD-PROGRAM CLAIMS → assume a parallel GJB2 filing is pending (18-month publication lag) and **SET A DOCKET WATCH ON THE ASSIGNEE** so we see it the week it publishes, not the week an investor raises it. **(2) THE PROTOCOL IS MATERIALLY SMALLER AND SOFTER THAN THE PRESS RELEASE READS — this LOWERS the threat level.** NCT07627971 (SONIX): **enrollment = TEN subjects**; **study start 2026-05-28** (so the 08-11 "first patient dosed" came ~10 WEEKS after the trial opened, not at go-live); primary completion **2027-12**, completion **2028-06**; status RECRUITING; sponsor Skylark Bio Inc., **no collaborators listed**; has_results false. **PRIMARY ENDPOINT IS SAFETY ONLY** — "AEs as it relates to the gene therapy (SKY-GJB2) and/or to the administration procedure (including the delivery device system (SKY-CAT))", through ~1 year. **Change in ABR intensity threshold (dB HL) is a SECONDARY endpoint.** So the guided **year-end-2026 "preliminary data" = safety + a handful of ABR thresholds out of a ten-child open-label study** — a real FIH signal, NOT an efficacy verdict. **Use: do not let it be treated as an efficacy verdict in a Series A conversation this fall in EITHER direction, and do not over-brace for it.** Eligibility narrows the beachhead further: **biallelic** pathogenic/likely-pathogenic GJB2 variants; bilateral SNHL with **≥85 dB HL at ≥1 frequency 500–4000 Hz in the treated ear**; ages **9 months–7 years**; **measurable ABR response required** (no-response excluded); autosomal-DOMINANT GJB2 excluded; bilateral cochlear implants excluded. = **severe-to-profound RECESSIVE PEDIATRIC** disease only, whereas **Sensorion claims THREE populations for SENS-601 including ADULTS with early-onset presbycusis**. **Durable read: GJB2 will FRAGMENT by severity and age rather than be won in one shot — there is real ground left, which softens the 08-18 "lane got materially more crowded" line.** Sites (3): **Massachusetts Eye and Ear**, Boston (= the institution of Skylark advisor **Daniel Lee** — anchor site is the advisor's own shop); **Hearts for Hearing**, Oklahoma City (**René Gifford, PhD** — major CI/audiology KOL); **Lehigh Valley Hospital–Cedar Crest**, Allentown PA (**Ravi N. Samy, MD FACS**). **(3) THERE IS A DELIVERY DEVICE AND WE HAVE NO ANSWER TO IT — the genuinely NEW competitive axis.** The protocol names the **SKY-CAT**, a proprietary intracochlear delivery catheter, **carrying its OWN secondary endpoint** ("Performance of the SKY-CAT delivery device… safety and effectiveness of the device in delivering the gene therapy", 1 month post-op). **SKY-GJB2 is a COMBINATION PRODUCT: Skylark will own clinical data on the cassette AND on the surgical access route.** In the ear, delivery may prove as defensible as the payload, and it is a moat with **nothing to do with expression control** — i.e. orthogonal to everything SELEXON contests. **Action (Geoff Lynn + Hugh Wells): what is Aletira's delivery answer in the ear, and is it someone else's device whose label someone else will own?** **INVESTOR GAP FILLED (our file said "funding/investors NOT disclosed"): the investor is ARKIN (Arkin Bio / Arkin Capital, Israel), position described on Arkin's own portfolio page as "NOTE to Series A", company listed as Boston MA, status Active.** Arkin's program description: "SKY-GjB2-001… a potentially FIRST- AND BEST-IN-CLASS gene therapy employing **targeted MICRO-DOSE** given as a single unilateral intracochlear administration." **Two reads: (a) the company that just dosed a child in our lead indication is EARLY-STAGE FINANCED (note-to-Series-A), not a well-capitalised juggernaut — so we are NOT outgunned on capital, we are OUTPACED ON THE CLOCK; (b) their safety pitch leans on GIVING LESS VIRUS (micro-dose + minimal systemic exposure), which is a PARTIAL SUBSTITUTE for restricting expression — that is the counter-argument Aletira will be positioned against in a bake-off, and it needs a prepared answer (dose reduction does not fix expression in the WRONG cell type).** People: **Will J. Also **zelasudil (RXC007)**, selective ROCK2, completing Ph2 in IPF (for partnering), + preclinical **DDR** program (IND 2027). **TWO-SIDED SO-WHAT: (a) VALIDATING — a syndicate just put $125M behind "gut-restricted oral small molecule in Crohn's" as a fundable, de-riskable ARCHITECTURE, and demonstrated the restriction IN HUMANS; cite it in Hemaka's deck, it is a citation not a landscape note. (b) NARROWING, and this is the durable line — CROHN'S IS BEING STAKED AXIS BY AXIS: inflammation long spoken for (TL1A, IL-23, integrin-α4β7, S1P, JAK), and as of 08-14 FIBROSIS is spoken for too, with money AND Fast Track behind it. NOTHING in the Fibrx package touches PAIN. So the standing instruction — differentiate on the enteric-nervous-system PAIN axis or the positioning is not defensible — is no longer merely true; it now describes the LAST OPEN AXIS in the indication. Build the positioning on that sentence and stop hedging it.** **STRUCTURAL FOOTNOTE for Eddie:** the route to Nasdaq here is a **reverse merger into a shell with CVRs** paying legacy holders out of the old assets — **17 such deals in 2026, the most since 2018** (Avere's $820M raise leading) — a live EXIT TEMPLATE for a Phase-2-ready Baltimore asset that does not want a conventional offering. **THIRD ITEM (Aletira thesis + a NEW diligence question; NEJM letter published LATE JULY, the MOLECULAR detail worked through by the neurology trade press 2026-08-18 — flag the date honestly, this is not a fresh primary source): Samelson-Jones B et al. (Children's Hospital of Philadelphia), "Cardiotoxic Effects and Microdystrophin Expression after Gene Therapy for DMD," NEJM 2026;395(6):615-617 (DOI 10.1056/NEJMc2518477, PMID 42485644; NEJM itself is Cloudflare/CAPTCHA-blocked to fetching — read via NeurologyLive through r.jina.ai).** A **17-year-old** with DMD given **delandistrogene moxeparvovec (Elevidys)** developed **myocarditis with symptomatic atrial fibrillation + mild thrombotic microangiopathy 2 DAYS after infusion**, then **recurrent troponin I elevations and worsening cardiac function ~DAY 42**, treated with IV methylprednisolone. **THE MOLECULAR FINDING IS THE POINT: cardiac tissue had HIGHER VECTOR COPY NUMBER than skeletal muscle — the vector ARRIVED, in quantity — yet immunofluorescence found microdystrophin in only ~5% of CARDIAC fibers and ~10% of SKELETAL fibers, and Western blot put microdystrophin BELOW 1% of normal dystrophin in BOTH tissues. Transcripts were UNEVENLY DISTRIBUTED across the cassette — far more from the 5' end than the 3' end — which the authors read as INCOMPLETE PACKAGING of the transgene cassette into the capsid** (compromising stable episome formation and sustained expression). Context: **12 myocarditis cases** after this product in WHO VigiAccess + FDA FAERS; TMA is an emerging rAAV toxicity seen across systemically administered rAAV but **not previously reported after Elevidys**. Authors: "This case highlights the need for safety monitoring beyond that recommended on the label." **SUPPORTING READ (deeper than our current pitch — adopt it): a field that scores itself on BIODISTRIBUTION just published a case where DELIVERY SUCCEEDED AND EXPRESSION FAILED. We have been arguing "off-target expression causes toxicity"; this licenses the more fundamental argument that YOU DO NOT CONTROL EXPRESSION MERELY BECAUSE YOU CONTROLLED DELIVERY, and the expression layer is where the therapeutic lives or dies.** **COUNTER-READ — THE REASON TO BRIEF IT, and a NEW open diligence item: the mechanism here is CASSETTE LENGTH + PACKAGING INTEGRITY.** A cassette pushed to the AAV packaging limit yields truncated genomes; truncated genomes yield 5'-biased transcripts; you get vector everywhere and protein nowhere. **SELEXON ADDS SEQUENCE TO A CASSETTE.** So the question a gene-therapy-literate investor WILL ask in the Series A: **what does a selective exon cost us in KILOBASES, and what is our FULL-vs-EMPTY capsid and GENOME-INTEGRITY data on a SELEXON-containing vector at the intended dose?** **Action (Avi + Hugh Wells): hold that number and that assay BEFORE the question arrives.** A splice element is small and the answer is probably fine — but "probably fine" is not a data-room answer. (Also consistent with the 08-18 regulatory reframe: rigorous on evidence, flexible on path.) **1104HEALTH (docket re-audited directly this morning via regulations.gov v4 API): NO MOVEMENT — still EIGHT total comments on HHSIG-2026-0034**, most recent posted **2026-08-14**, and **still NOT ONE from a trade association or major sponsor**. **TIMING NOTE / partial correction to the 08-18 line: the Federal Register notice states comments due 5:00 p.m. ET Monday 2026-08-24, while the regulations.gov document record lists commentEndDate 2026-08-25. Operative instruction: WORK TO MONDAY AT 5 P.M. Future sweeps must paginate by 30 and go past cursor 240 on heavy days (08-11 had 281 records). **LEAD (full text READ, 144KB via r.jina.ai on the v1.full page — lead-eligible) = Michael D. Kornberg lab, Johns Hopkins Neurology (Peter A. Calabresi on author list — top-tier MS KOL), "Remodeling oligodendrocyte lipid metabolism via liver X receptors overcomes inflammatory blockade of remyelination," bioRxiv v1 posted 2026-08-13 (DOI 10.64898/2026.08.07.743529).** Science: interferon-γ in MS lesions reprograms oligodendrocyte-precursor-cell (OPC) lipid metabolism (synthesis→fatty-acid oxidation), depleting the free fatty acids OPCs need to mature into myelinating cells and flipping them into an aberrant immune-like antigen-presenting state = mechanistic explanation for remyelination failure. Restoring lipids (oleic acid / DGAT1 or FAO block) reverses it; **pharmacologic liver X receptor (LXR) activation** (tool agonists GW3965 + structurally distinct T0901317) rebalances lipid metabolism, suppresses the immune-like phenotype, and overcomes the IFN-γ differentiation block in BOTH mouse AND human hPSC-derived OPCs (MBP-luciferase reporter); metabolic signature confirmed in human surgical OL-lineage cells + human MS-lesion snRNA-seq. In vivo (adoptive-transfer-cuprizone model where inflammation directly blocks repair) LXR agonist 10 mg/kg IP started 3 days post-transfer (deliberately AFTER immune infiltration, so benefit is repair-side not immunosuppression — confirmed minimal effect on CNS immune infiltrate) increased mature oligodendrocytes (Olig2+ASPA+) + myelin (fluoromyelin). **COI VERBATIM (clean — the enabling tell): "MDK has received consulting fees from Genentech, TG Therapeutics, and OptumRx on topics unrelated to this manuscript. Other authors declare that they have no competing interests."** = NO equity, NO company, NO patent on this biology. Funding all society/foundation: National MS Society (RFA-2312-42505, JF-2407-43562, FG-2308-42298), Race to Erase MS, Wieden Family Foundation, JHU Catalyst Award, Karen Toffler Charitable Trust, Torrey MS Endowment, Maryland Stem Cell Research Fund (2025-R2-MSCRFD-00045), Gilbert Family Foundation → OPEN, unencumbered JHTV license path (Blackbird sweet spot). **Blackbird read (LEAD — clears the bar well): remyelination = marquee validated whitespace in MS (no approved remyelinating therapy; DMTs stop new damage but leave disability); fresh mechanism + human validation + in-vivo repair + clean IP + Kornberg-Calabresi founding-caliber team. CANDID CATCH: mechanism/target-validation paper, NOT a molecule (tool compounds only), and LXR has a KNOWN liability — broad LXRα activation drives hepatic steatosis + hypertriglyceridemia (stalled prior LXR programs). The company is "build/in-license a brain-penetrant, liver-sparing LXR modulator against a validated rationale" — paper notes LXRβ is the isotype expressed in OPCs = natural handle for a selective/liver-sparing molecule; that selectivity is both the central risk AND the moat.** Actions: Avi — is a β-selective CNS-penetrant LXR modulator tractable medchem + who has chemical matter to in-license around; Eddie — open JHTV field-of-use conversation now (provisional-filing moment). Added Kornberg/Calabresi LXR-remyelination-in-MS to tracked-PI/lead list (see §2b). **SECONDARY (DEFERRED lead from 08-09 now CLOSED as watch-not-build) = Jennifer Elisseeff lab, Johns Hopkins, "Senescent cell networks link matrix remodeling and vascular dysfunction in human fibroids," bioRxiv v1 2026-08-07 (DOI 10.64898/2026.08.06.743362) — full text NOW RENDERED (157KB) and read.** First single-cell "senotype" map of human fibroids (senescent cells concentrate in mural ~61% / fibroblast / fibroid-expanded endothelial compartments, drive ECM remodeling via collagen VI + impaired vessel maturation via TEAD4/YAP mechanosensing); translational hook = in tissue from a JHU Phase I injectable-collagenase fibroid trial (Singh/Borahay), collagenase reduced both ECM density + p16+ senescent burden. Huge underserved market (fibroids 70-80% of women; leading cause of hysterectomy; little non-surgical). **WATCH not build:** IP thin/derivative (descriptive atlas; the actionable therapy = collagenase, already clinical + commoditized = Endo's collagenase clostridium histolyticum; the novel TEAD4/COL6A3 mechanosensing driver is a hypothesis). **COI HEAVY (encumbrance flag): J.H.E. holds equity in Unity Biotechnology + Aegeria Soft Tissue, consultant for Tessara; E.J.F. (Fertig, UMB) SAB/consulting incl. familial relationship to a founder of PushCART Therapeutics** — serial senescence/ECM founder, so fibroid-senescence IP likely reads onto existing vehicles. Previously verified 2026-08-02 (Sunday two-episode day — Sourcing Radar + Portfolio Watch). **SOURCING RADAR:** the week (2026-07-27→08-02) broke a long thin run with one fresh, FULL-TEXT-READ, company-shaped UMB oncology preprint. Corresponding-author bioRxiv/medRxiv sweep (1240 bioRxiv + 387 medRxiv via api.biorxiv.org details API, JHU/UMB/Lieber/KKI affiliation regex, UMB word-boundaried) returned 11 bioRxiv + 6 medRxiv hits; all but one were methods/tools/basic-neuro/clinical-epi with no asset. **LEAD (full text read via r.jina.ai direct on the v1.full page — 88KB body rendered; the helper fell through to API-abstract-only [4KB], and PIPELINE's jina channel remains broken, so curl r.jina.ai directly worked) = Feyruz V. Rassool lab, UMB School of Medicine / Marlene and Stewart Greenebaum Comprehensive Cancer Center, "STING agonists in combination with epigenetic drugs potentiate ZNFX1-driven inflammatory necroptosis in TP53-mutated AML," bioRxiv v1 2026-07-28 (DOI 10.64898/2026.07.27.741029; cancer biology).** COI VERBATIM: "The authors declare no potential conflicts of interest" = NO filed patent disclosed by UMB. Co-authors incl. **Stephen B. Baylin + Michael J. Topper (JHU epigenetics / viral-mimicry school)** + Maria R. Baer (UMB Greenebaum leukemia) + Kenneth P. Nephew (Indiana). Funding: NCI P30 CA134274 (Greenebaum CCC), NCI SPORE P50CA254897, Maryland Cigarette Restitution Fund, Van Andel–SU2C Epigenetics Dream Team. **Science:** TP53-mut AML (~10% de novo / 25% therapy-related; dismal on chemo/transplant; ven+DAC barely helps this subgroup; NO established immunotherapy). Finding = losing p53 makes AML cells MORE sensitive to a STING agonist (previously undescribed vulnerability). Next-gen STING agonist **C92 (Curadev Pharma — human-specific all-variants, IV-dosable, allosteric non-CDN, blocks STING proton channel; DONATED by Curadev, CEO Arjun Surya/CSO Monali Banerjee)** potentiates STING activation + cytokine release (CXCL10/TNFα/IFNγ/IFI27) in TP53-KO vs WT; effect generalizes to ADU-S100/MSA-2/diABZI but C92 superior. Adding generic DNMTi **decitabine (DAC)** synergizes (SynergyFinder/Chou-Talalay) — transcriptome-wide repeat-element (viral-mimicry) induction + IFN gene program. Killing = TP53-INDEPENDENT inflammatory NECROPTOSIS via ZNFX1→ZBP1→pRIPK3/MLKL (double KO of TP53+ZNFX1 abrogates), NOT apoptosis; ZNFX1 = Rassool's recently-reported STING/mito master regulator (Cancer Res 2025). Humanized NSG-SGM3 (CD34+) MOLM-14 mice: C92+DAC cuts leukemia burden, no weight loss, ↑plasma TNFα/CXCL10 + splenic CD8 infiltration. Authors call for STING-agonist+DNMTi trials in TP53-mut AML (+ extends to other TP53-mut cancers: ovarian/endometrial/lung) and STING+checkpoint combos. **Blackbird read (CANDID — watch-and-partner, NOT found-and-lead):** biology strong + p53-mutant biomarker = exactly the patient-selection story the STING-agonist class has lacked (1st-gen agonists failed: intratumoral, mouse-STING). BUT the ownable IP is USE + BIOMARKER + COMBINATION only — decitabine is generic, C92 composition-of-matter is CURADEV's (donated), and no UMB patent is disclosed. So it's a partnering/co-development play with whoever owns the STING agonist, not a composition-of-matter newco (parallels the 07-22 Raabe/corin "use-not-composition" pattern). Value = a differentiated biomarker-stratified indication + the Baltimore JHU(Baylin/Topper)×UMB(Rassool/Baer) epigenetic-immuno-oncology axis on the co-investment surface. **Actions:** Eddie — does UM Ventures intend to file on method+biomarker, and is the natural home a Curadev co-dev deal (is Curadev already running C92 into AML, or is that lane open)? Avi/Yixuan — what's ownable/defensible on a use+biomarker claim when you don't control the molecule; FTO vs Curadev's C92 estate. Lower dealability than the 07-25/07-26 Suk aCD47-CpG lead; higher scientific conviction on the biology. Added Feyruz V. Rassool + Maria R. Baer + the STING-agonist/DNMTi/ZNFX1-necroptosis-in-TP53-mut-AML target to tracked-PI/target list; Curadev Pharma + C92 to tracked-company/asset list. **What else crossed (NOT leads, logged so we don't re-chase):** Chulan Kwon JHU SF3B2-splicing-factor cardiomyocyte-calcium-handling (10.64898/2026.07.27.740735, developmental biology — target biology, no asset; Kwon lab already tracked for iPSC-cardiomyocyte bioincubation); JHU tool/methods preprints (Timothy Harris Neuropixels-1536 probe; Mao-Jan Lin ImpuT2T pangenome-patching; Gudmundson real-time MRI-AI; D.B. Previously verified 2026-07-28 (Tuesday — Portfolio Watch only). **One substantive fresh in-window item, and it lands directly on a Blackbird
platform. LEAD = CAMP4 Therapeutics CMP-002 cleared for first-in-human (07-27), direct validation of the RNA translation-activator /
upregulation modality.** Australia's TGA + local HREC cleared CAMP4 to start a Phase 1/2 FIH trial of CMP-002, an ASO that works OPPOSITE
to knockdown ASOs — it targets regulatory RNA (regRNA) upstream of a gene to release the brake and RAISE target-protein expression. Target
SYNGAP1; indication SYNGAP1-related disorder (rare haploinsufficient neurodevelopmental condition, >10,000 US pts, universal ID + ~85%
epilepsy, no approved DMT); intrathecal. Preclinical: dose-dependent SYNGAP protein up in patient neurons, phenotype reversal in humanized
haploinsufficient mice, seizure reduction, broad brain distribution in NHP. Financing: clearance triggered 2nd close of a Sept-2025 SPA =
up to $50M (Janus Henderson, Vivo, 5AM, Balyasny, Adage, Coastlands, Trails Edge, CURE SYNGAP1). RAP Platform maps regRNAs → ASOs across
>1,200 haploinsufficient / partial-LOF disorders. **So-what:** clearest validation to date of the "turn-the-protein-up" lane our RNA
translation-activator platform (§2) lives in — knockdown drugs don't apply to LOF/haploinsufficiency; CAMP4 (the named competitor in that
lane) has now (a) shown a regulator will let RNA-upregulation into humans and (b) framed the >1,200-disorder commercial prize we should be
framing. Candid read cuts both ways: VALIDATION (not a threat to a specific asset — we have no competing SYNGAP1 program) BUT a PACE-SETTER
= the leading independent RNA-upregulation company is now FIH + freshly funded, so the window to stake a differentiated wedge (targeting
mechanism vs regRNA-ASO; delivery vs intrathecal; disease set) is a live clock. **Action logged: Avi + platform leads define the defensible
angle now.** Added CMP-002/CAMP4 FIH datapoint to §2 RNA translation-activator entry. Other threads NO CHANGE since 07-27 (kept to one-line

and assessment of long-acting pharmaceutical co-formulated crystals," Int J Pharm 702:127215, DOI
10.1016/j.ijpharm.2026.127215; corr. Joshua C. Doloff jcdoloff@jhu.edu, JHU BME + Translational

Ophthalmology) + Daiheon Lee + Gijung Kwak + Jung Soo Suk (senior corresponding; jsuk@som.umaryland.edu),
Department of Neurosurgery + University of Maryland Medicine Institute for Neuroscience Discovery
(UM-MIND), University of Maryland School of Medicine (Suk ALSO holds JHU Ophthalmology + JHU Neurosurgery
+ UMD College Park Fischell Bioengineering appointments — he came up through the JHU Center for
Nanomedicine cluster [cf. the tracked Wilmer + JHU BME + CNM cluster] and is now building at UMB's new
UM-MIND institute = concrete ecosystem-migration intel, and a rare JHU→UMB move onto the UM Ventures side).
"An immune-stimulating antibody conjugate spatiotemporally targeting CD47 and TLR9 elicits macrophage-
dependent tumor clearance and durable anti-cancer adaptive immunity," *bioRxiv* v1 2026-07-23 (DOI
10.64898/2026.07.22.739359; cancer biology). **COI verbatim: "J.S.S. and S.W.C. are inventors on a patent
application related to the aCD47-CpG conjugate technology described in this manuscript. The remaining
authors declare no competing interests."** Funding: NIH R01NS119609 (J.S.S.); **Maryland Innovation
Initiative (MII) of TEDCO, award 0722-005 (J.S.S.)** = Maryland state translational capital, precisely the
Blackbird/Maryland ecosystem; Sejong Science Fellowship NRF Korea (B.K.); Greenebaum CCC flow core.
**Science:** CD47 = ubiquitous "don't-eat-me" macrophage checkpoint; anti-CD47 mAbs (magrolimab/Gilead —
note AML Ph3 magrolimab+ven+aza failed on risk/benefit; ALX Oncology; Pfizer/Trillium) drive phagocytosis
but clearance is "immunologically silent" → no durable adaptive immunity, recurrence. aCD47-CpG covalently
links anti-CD47 (mouse MIAP410 / human B6H12) to a TLR9-agonist CpG oligo via non-cleavable Sulfo-SMCC,
CpG 3′-conjugated to preserve potency; DAR 1–4 screened, DAR 3 optimal (NF-κB plateaus beyond DAR 3).
Core insight = TLR9 is restricted to the endosome/phagolysosome — the SAME compartment where engulfed
tumor antigen is processed — so the conjugate guarantees the identical macrophage that engulfs the tumor
(CD47 blockade) receives the intracellular co-stim signal, a spatiotemporal coupling you CANNOT get by
co-dosing two free drugs. Conjugate shows an initial phagocytosis delay (attenuated CD47 affinity) then
surpasses naked aCD47 after ~8h (TLR9-driven sustained phagocytosis); upregulates CD80/CD86/CD40 + iNOS
(M1) + TNFα/IL-6/IL-12; boosts MHC-I cross-presentation + OT-I CD8 proliferation + IFN-γ (naked aCD47 does
NOT). In vivo: MTD 10 mg/kg; tumor-dominant biodistribution despite CD47 sinks; human Raji NHL xenograft →
profound regression ABOLISHED by clodronate macrophage depletion (macrophage-dependent), M1:M2 shift only
with conjugate; immunocompetent systemic A20 NHL → reduced burden + extended survival + complete day-106
rechallenge resistance (durable memory); orthotopic syngeneic 4T1 TNBC (cold) → tumor + lung-metastasis
suppression, enriched macrophages/monocytes/CD8/effector+central-memory T cells, Treg depletion; human
MDA-MB-231 TNBC recapitulated. **Blackbird read (STRONG — strongest UMB lead of the month):** (1)
Differentiation — every clinical ISAC (Bolt Biotherapeutics BDC-1001 + cohort) hangs a TLR7/8/9/STING
payload off a tumor-specific antibody (HER2/EGFR/CD22) and needs FcγR engagement = antigen-restricted;
aCD47-CpG rides a ubiquitous pan-cancer myeloid checkpoint, lowers phagocytic threshold intrinsically,
works Fc-INDEPENDENTLY (potent on mouse IgG1 w/ minimal activating-FcγR affinity) → enables an Fc-silent
backbone to cut systemic toxicity; antigen-agnostic + Fc-independent = differentiated platform position
(vs the recent aCD47-listeriolysin-O conjugate that needs cytosolic release). (2) Dealability — LIVE
patent application (Suk + Chung inventors) + TEDCO/MII state funding = open UM Ventures licensing window
NOW. (3) Ecosystem — nanomedicine builder from the JHU CNM cluster now on the UM Ventures × Blackbird
co-investment surface alongside tracked Chao (TLR7-ITGAM) + Martin (MCAM/TetherChip). **Actions:** Eddie —
UM Ventures filing status + is Suk contemplating a vehicle + BioHub/BioVentures fit at this preclinical
stage. Avi/Yixuan — freedom-to-operate vs the anti-CD47 antibody estate + ISAC linker patents (conjugate
chemistry = where value AND risk sit); Fc-silencing + human-conjugate manufacturability/CMC. Added Jung
Soo Suk to tracked-PI list + new §2b "Suk lab UMB Neurosurgery/UM-MIND aCD47-CpG CD47×TLR9 immune-
stimulating antibody conjugate platform (antigen-agnostic, Fc-independent; NHL + TNBC; live patent +
TEDCO/MII funding)" entry. **Pick 2 (ABSTRACT-ONLY — Cancer Letters Elsevier-paywalled [ScienceDirect
returns only JS, no PMC], NOT a full read; flag-and-chase) — Ferrer lab UMB Greenebaum GSTT1/CD133/FGFR
PDAC stem-like axis.** Deborah de la Caridad Delgado Herrera (first) … Elana J. Fertig (co-author; already
tracked) + Christina M. Ferrer (senior corresponding; cferrer@som.umaryland.edu), UMB School of Medicine +
Marlene and Stewart Greenebaum Comprehensive Cancer Center + UMB Institute for Genome Sciences. "GSTT1
promotes stemness and FGFR inhibitor sensitivity in pancreatic cancer through regulation of CD133 (PROM1),"
*Cancer Letters* 2026 in press (DOI 10.1016/j.canlet.2026.218733; edat 2026-07-24). **Science (abstract):**
a slow-cycling, highly metastatic GSTT1+ PDAC subpopulation is stem-like (tumor-sphere formation, PROM1/
CD133, Wnt+FGF); CD133+GSTT1+ human cells define a maximal stem-like state; FGFR-inhibitor sensitivity is
context-dependent (tumor-sphere conditions only); FGFR3 tracks GSTT1/CD133; GSTT1 knockdown lowers CD133 +
alters FGFR-inhibitor sensitivity; in patient-derived organoids, GSTT1/PROM1 co-expression predicts
enhanced response to the approved multi-kinase inhibitor nintedanib. **Blackbird read:** GSTT1 is germline-
polymorphic (common whole-gene null) = ready-made patient-stratification biomarker; commercial shape =
companion-biomarker + repurposed-approved-drug (nintedanib) play in PDAC on the UM Ventures × Blackbird
surface, NOT a new molecule. Diligence: whether Greenebaum filed on the biomarker; lower conviction than

tracked list (Fertig already tracked via Popel-Fertig-Deshpande spatial-QSP + MInt-HDX). **Watch (ABSTRACT-
ONLY — body not yet rendered, DEFERRAL to re-read) — Watanabe/Bergles JHU "skoupocytosis."** Rachel Pepper
(first) … Dwight E. Bergles + Shigeki Watanabe (senior corresponding), JHU (Cell Biology / Neuroscience).
"Microglia extract neuronal proteolytic organelles via skoupocytosis," *bioRxiv* v1 2026-07-24 (DOI
10.64898/2026.07.22.740182). **Science (abstract):** microglia transiently contact neuronal membranes and
pinch off a small portion of the process containing an oversized proteolytic organelle (rest left intact) —
a new clearance route ("skoupocytosis," Greek for garbage) bypassing retrograde organelle transport; PS
lipase ABHD16a accumulates at these sites, converting phosphatidylserine → lyso-PS to initiate it (traces
to a JHU Cell Biology 2024 seminar "a role for homeostatic microglia in synaptic proteostasis"; no newsroom
release yet). **Blackbird read:** no asset, no disclosed IP, body not rendered (couldn't read in full) —
but a defined druggable enzyme (ABHD16a) gating neuronal proteostasis is seed-grade neurodegeneration
target biology. Target-biology watch + deferral; added Shigeki Watanabe + ABHD16a/skoupocytosis to

sourcing FLAG — NEWSROOM/ABSTRACT-GROUNDED, PNAS body PAYWALLED, NOT a full read) — DCTPP1 inhibitors
as a decitabine resistance-breaker.** Glenn Hauk (first) + Jianyong Liu + William G. Nelson (Kimmel
Cancer Center director) + Srinivasan "Vasan" Yegnasubramanian (co-corresponding; Oncology/Pathology/
Radiation Oncology; director, inHealth precision medicine; syegnasu@jhmi.edu) + James M. Berger
(co-corresponding; director, JHM Institute for Basic Biomedical Sciences; jmberger@jhmi.edu), all JHU
School of Medicine + Sidney Kimmel Comprehensive Cancer Center. "Structural and cellular insights into
DCTPP1 antagonists and their synergistic action with DNMT inhibitors," *PNAS* 123(25):e2534029123,
published 2026-06-15 (DOI 10.1073/pnas.2534029123; PMID 42296362; PDB 9YSM–9YSP). **Vetting gate note:
PNAS body subscription-gated — isOpenAccess=N, not in PMC/EPMC, no preprint (EPMC MED-only record); no
biorxiv preprint of this exact work (the 2024 bioRxiv "triptolide/DCTPP1" is a DIFFERENT paper).
Briefed from the authors' full Significance section + Abstract + Johns Hopkins/EurekAlert press release
(dated July 15, 2026; hopkinsmedicine /2026/07/ + eurekalert 1136235 + medicalxpress) — treated as a
flag-and-chase lead, explicitly NOT presented as a full-text deep read, to honor PIPELINE full-text
eligibility gate; hence not the formal full-text lead pick (that is Pick 2, read in full).**
**COI verbatim: "The authors have filed for a provisional patent related to this work."** Funding:
NCI R35-CA263778 (Berger); P50-CA272391 prostate SPORE (Yegnasubramanian); P30-CA006973 (Sidney Kimmel
CCC); Commonwealth Foundation + Irving Hansen Foundation + Prostate Cancer Foundation (Yegnasubramanian);
JHU ChemCore ran the HTS. **Science (per Significance+Abstract):** DCTPP1 = nucleotide pyrophosphatase
surveilling the dNTP pool, hydrolyzing deoxy-5-methylcytosine triphosphates + other modified nucleotides
to block misincorporation into DNA (preserves genomic stability + epigenetic programming). Because
nucleoside-analog DNMT inhibitors (decitabine, azacitidine) work BY misincorporation into DNA, DCTPP1
is a cell-intrinsic RESISTANCE factor degrading their efficacy → inhibit DCTPP1 to potentiate the drug.
10,000-compound HTS yielded existing + previously-unreported inhibitor classes at sub-micromolar
potency; X-ray crystallography solved THREE distinct classes bound in DCTPP1's nucleotide pocket
(contacting a tryptophan pair + two critical histidines mimicking substrate); biochemical assays w/
modified scaffolds confirmed interactions; cell-based = significant synergy of lead inhibitors +
decitabine blocking PROSTATE cancer cell growth; loss/gain-of-function confirmed DCTPP1-dependence.
**Blackbird thesis:** resistance-breaker layered on ALREADY-APPROVED oncology drugs (installed MDS/AML
hypomethylating-agent market + demonstrated solid-tumor/prostate path); novel-target + tool inhibitors
already at crystal-structure resolution + provisional patent ALREADY FILED (live JHTV window); NCE value
capture (the inhibitor) on a generic backbone; heavyweight founding/SAB nucleus (Berger structural
biology + IBBS director; Yegnasubramanian inHealth + prostate/epigenetics; Nelson Kimmel director).
Diligence gaps before real: chemical-matter NOVELTY + freedom-to-operate (authors describe BOTH new and
known inhibitor classes) + combination-drug regulatory/pricing story. **Actions:** Eddie — JHTV filing
status + whether Berger/Yegnasubramanian are contemplating a vehicle. Avi/Yixuan — pull full paper +
patent when it publishes; pressure-test chemistry novelty + FTO. Berger (already logged 07-21 as ORC–HP1
co-author, corr. Botchan) upgraded to senior-corresponding on an oncology druggable-target paper; add
Yegnasubramanian + Nelson + DCTPP1-inhibitor/decitabine-potentiation target to tracked-PI/target list.
**Pick 2 (fresh, FULL TEXT READ — the formal full-text pick; platform-watch/relationship, NOT a
spinout) — Jain lab JHU host-directed TB-meningitis therapy in a new human brain-organoid model.**
Carlos E. Ruiz-Gonzalez (first) + Medha Singh + … William R. Bishai + Sanjay K. Jain (senior
corresponding), JHU (Center for Infection and Inflammation Imaging Research). "Host-directed treatments
for tuberculous meningitis utilizing a multi-platform approach across mouse and human models," *Nat
Commun* 2026-07-21 (DOI 10.1038/s41467-026-75202-6; PMID 42481504; nature.com OA via jina proxy —
idp.nature.com auth-wall on direct fetch, not yet in PMC). **COI verbatim: "The authors declare no
competing interests."** Funding: NIH R01-AI145435-A1 + R01-AI153349 + R56-AI179012-A1 + R01-AI190038 +
S10-OD030381-A1 (all S.K.J.) + NIAID intramural. **Science:** TB-meningitis damage is CNS-neuroinflammation-
driven; steroids (dexamethasone) cut mortality ~25% but not neuro deficits. 12 immunomodulatory drugs
(11 classes; FDA-approved or in trials elsewhere) tested as isoniazid adjuncts across a mouse TB-meningitis
model (mortality/neuro-deficit/MRI endpoints) + a NEWLY-DEVELOPED immune-vascularized human brain organoid
model of TB meningitis + patient PBMCs. Bestatin (aminopeptidase inhibitor), imatinib (TKI), roflumilast
(PDE-4 inhibitor), and a PARP-1 inhibitor significantly reduced BOTH mortality and neuro deficits vs
the dexamethasone reference, via neuroinflammation suppression (abstract also named thalidomide/pomalidomide
+ semaglutide as outperforming SOC). **Blackbird read (CANDID):** drugs are repurposed generics (no COI,
no patent) + TB meningitis is a global-health indication (BARDA/foundation economics, not venture returns)
= NOT a drug spin-out. The ownable/licensable asset is the immune-vascularized human brain organoid
NEUROINFLAMMATION screening chassis — adjacent to tracked Baltimore imaging/model platforms (Walczak/Liang
MIPOT, Kalhor 3DEEP). Plus a relationship worth having (Jain/Bishai infectious-disease + molecular-imaging
group; Jain has imaging-tracer IP history). Log as platform-watch + relationship item; look if the organoid
model is spun out/licensed as a neuroinflammation screening tool. Add Sanjay K. Jain (JHU CIIIR) to
tracked-PI list as neuroinflammation brain-organoid-platform lead.

FULL TEXT READ)** — Jaso-Vera ME first + Ruan X senior corresponding (Division of Endocrinology,
Diabetes & Metabolism, Johns Hopkins School of Medicine + Institute for Fundamental Biomedical
Research, Johns Hopkins All Children's Hospital, St. Petersburg FL), *PLoS One* 21(7):e0353674,
2026-07-21 (DOI 10.1371/journal.pone.0353674; PMID 42479790; open access), "hLMR1, a
hepatocyte-specific long noncoding RNA that represses amino acid catabolism through pre-mRNA
interaction in human liver." **COI verbatim "The authors have no relevant financial or
non-financial interests to disclose"; NO patents mentioned = JHTV provisional window likely still
open.** Funding: JHU institutional + AHA 23SCEFIA1156649 (Ruan) + AHA 24DIVSUP1291162 (Jaso-Vera)
+ JHACH ACRI fellowship (Takaoka) + NIH DK099257 (Soto-Gutierrez, Pitt) + Pitt Center for
Transcriptional Medicine. **Science:** hLMR1 = "human liver metabolic regulator 1," a
hepatocyte-specific AND human-specific lncRNA acting as a BRAKE on hepatic amino-acid catabolism +
ureagenesis — the pathways impaired-with-no-prior-molecular-explanation in MASLD/MASH. Humanized-liver
mouse (human hepatocytes 15–80% of liver by circulating human albumin): knockdown broadly upregulates
AA-degradation genes (SDS, GLS2, AASS) + urea-cycle enzymes (ASS1, CPS1, ARG1). Mechanism = hLMR1
base-pairs to the pre-mRNAs of those genes via a 12-nt complementary motif (nt 587–598);
full-length overexpression completely blocks glucagon-induced SDS/CPS1/HGD, Δ587–598 mutant has
NO effect. Human relevance: expression climbs healthy→MASH reciprocal to SDS/ASS1; one-week
low-carb intervention lowers hLMR1 + raises catabolic enzymes. **Blackbird thesis:** first-in-class
(not me-too) liver-restricted human-specific REPRESSOR RNA → you win by knocking it down; authors
explicitly nominate ASO or GalNAc-conjugated siRNA (verbatim: "pharmacological suppression of
hLMR1—using antisense oligonucleotides or GalNAc-conjugated siRNAs—could simultaneously ameliorate
hyperlipidemia and hyperglycemia") = the de-risked Ionis/Alnylam GalNAc-to-liver playbook pouring
drug into exactly and only the cell type this target lives in. MASH field (resmetirom/Madrigal
approved + FGF21/incretin crowd) ALL works lipid/fibrosis — nobody drugs hepatic NITROGEN
metabolism; AA/urea-cycle arm also opens a hyperammonemia / urea-cycle-disorder ORPHAN lane
(liver siRNA restoring ureagenesis = clean orphan story). Early (target validation in humanized
mouse, no therapeutic molecule yet) but right stage for a venture studio — found-a-company around
ownable foundational biology. **Actions:** Eddie — Ruan + JHTV filing-status conversation,

RCOR2 knockdown phenocopies corin + desensitizes cells = CoREST-complex target validation.

## 1. The organization

- **Blackbird Laboratories, Inc.** — nonprofit life-sciences accelerator,
  Baltimore. Makes translational grants into partner-institution research to
  de-risk academic IP toward startup formation.
- **Blackbird BioVentures** — for-profit venture arm; the early investor that
  participates when a program is ready to spin out.
- **Founding grant:** $100M from the Stephen and Renee Bisciotti Foundation
  (Steve Bisciotti owns the Baltimore Ravens; "Blackbird" ≈ Raven). Created
  Jan 2023, public launch Nov 2023.
- **Scale (as reported mid-2025):** ~18 companies backed; ~20 exploratory
  research projects; 4 licensed programs. ~75% of companies in Baltimore,
  ~95% in Maryland.
- **Blackbird BioHub** — ~35–42k sq ft wet-lab incubator at **City Garage**,
  Baltimore Peninsula (former bus depot). Private benches, office suites,
  shared instruments, on-site vivarium. Opened March 2026; helped by a $2M
  Maryland "Build Our Future" grant. Home base for incubated spinouts.

### Leadership
- **Matt Tremblay, Ph.D.** — CEO, founding board member. Ex-COO of Scripps
  Research and its drug-discovery arm Calibr. (Primary audience.)
- **Eddie Cherok, Ph.D.** — Chief Business Officer. Ex-Scripps/Calibr corp dev.
- **Hemaka Rajapakse, Ph.D.** — Venture Partner (project mgmt, NewCo
  leadership, scientific diligence).
- **Esther Park, Ph.D.** — Associate Director of Ventures (started as an
  inaugural Blackbird Fellow).
- **Maisha Rahman, Ph.D.** — Chief of Staff.
- **Avinash (Avi) Khanna, Ph.D.** — Sr. Director, Molecular Discovery.
- **Bridget Duvall** — Director, Lab Operations (ran BioHub buildout).

### Partner institutions
- **Johns Hopkins University / Johns Hopkins Medicine** — anchor partner.
  Tech-transfer: **Johns Hopkins Technology Ventures (JHTV)**, incl. the
  **FastForward** incubator. JHU Drug Discovery (Slusher lab) is a recurring
  source.
  - **JHU Life Sciences Research Initiative** (launched 2026-06-10): $80M/yr
    for two years of internal funding into translational/basic life sciences
    research. Two tracks — **Transformational Science Team Awards** ($40M/yr
    pool, awards start at $10M per project, 4 years of support, multi-PI,
    milestone-driven) and **High-Impact Individual Awards** ($40M/yr pool).
    Oversight committee chaired by **Ashani Weeraratna** (Bloomberg
    Distinguished Professor, Cancer Biology). Applications opened 2026-06-15.
    Direct tailwind for Blackbird sourcing — produces investor-ready preclinical
    packages on 6-18 month horizon. Worth Esther/Hemaka angling for visibility
    on the submitted-project longlist.
- **University of Maryland, Baltimore (UMB)** — via **UM Ventures, Baltimore**
  (co-investment agreement with Blackbird; "MPower" branding).
- **Lieber Institute for Brain Development (LIBD)** — neuropsychiatry; affiliated
  with JHU. Source of the schizophrenia program.

### Programs (talent pipeline)
- **Blackbird Fellows** — paid (~10 hrs/wk) fellowships for near-graduation JHU
  School of Medicine Ph.D. students doing investment due diligence; inaugural
  oncology-focused cohort: **Sarah Neshat, Esther Park, Brittni Moore** (each
  assigned an oncology modality to build investment criteria). Park converted to
  full-time Ventures; Moore moved into biotech/pharma strategy & BD.
- EIR-style company-creation track converting academic founders into operators.
