  Disrupting ITGAM-Dependent Immune-Endothelial Interactions," *JACC: Basic to
  Translational Science* 11(8):101626, DOI 10.1016/j.jacbts.2026.101626, PMID
  42447539, published online 2026-07-14. Open access. **IP disclosure verbatim:**
  "A patent application related to use of TLR7/8 antagonist for treatment of cardiac
  ischemia–reperfusion injury has been filed (PCT/US2025/037133, 'Methods of Treating
  Ischemia/Reperfusion Injury'). The authors have reported that they have no
  relationships relevant to the contents of this paper to disclose." Funding: NIH
  R35-GM140822 (Chao); AHA 26POST1559348 (Li).
- **Mechanism.** Mouse LAD-ligation IR model, 45 min ischemia + 24 h reperfusion.
  Genetic TLR7 KO + pharmacological inhibition with **enpatoran** (Merck KGaA-developed
  selective TLR7/8 antagonist; MEK KGaA is pursuing enpatoran clinically in **systemic
  lupus erythematosus + cutaneous lupus erythematosus + myositis**) both cut infarct
  size (MI/AAR ratio) non-additively — epistasis confirms target. snRNA-seq localizes
  mechanism to TLR7-driven inflammatory endothelial program + coordinated myeloid
  program, both converging on **ITGAM (CD11b, α_M integrin)** upregulation. Anti-ITGAM
  antibody (Bio X Cell BE0007) blockade phenocopies TLR7 KO — significantly smaller
  infarct + markedly reduced neutrophil + monocyte infiltration + epistasis with
  TLR7 loss.
- **Effect sizes.** Enpatoran 5 mg/kg IP reduces MI/AAR by **35-76%** across four
  dosing windows: prevention (1 h pre-ischemia), treatment (35 min during ischemia),
  early rescue (30 min post-ischemia), late rescue (2 h post-ischemia). Prevention +
  early-rescue windows work; late-rescue efficacy starts falling off. Peri-procedural
  PCI (stent placement + related percutaneous coronary intervention) is the clinical
  use case; the dosing-window story maps cleanly to a peri-procedural Phase 2 protocol.
- **Blackbird sourcing significance — direct sourcing lead.** UMB SoM methods-of-use
  IP (PCT/US2025/037133) on a cardio-reperfusion indication where enpatoran's
  autoimmune-indication development at Merck KGaA is de-risking the tox package.
  Cross-licensing leverage: enpatoran composition-of-matter is Merck KGaA's, but
  UMB owns methods-of-use for cardio-IR. Whoever wants to develop enpatoran into cardio
  needs a UMB license, and UMB can shop the MoU claim to any TLR7-only or TLR7/8
  inhibitor competitor program broadly. Sits directly on the UM Ventures × Blackbird
  BioVentures co-investment surface — same track that produced aSKY. Peri-procedural
  anti-inflammatory adjunct slots into every interventional-cardiology franchise
  (Bristol Myers Squibb, Novartis Cardiovascular, CSL Behring). Chao's R35 =
  maximum-security academic funding + STAR = deepest translational-anesthesiology
  infrastructure in the US.
- **Diligence questions.** (1) TLR7 blockade impact on acute viral defense in
  peri-procedural window — short duration probably fine; needs immunopharmacology
  characterization. (2) Whether MoU claims cover competitor TLR7-only or TLR7/8
  molecules broadly, or are narrowed to enpatoran specifically. (3) Large-animal
  or human ex-vivo replication of the ITGAM/CD11b axis before Phase 2 — reperfusion
  biology varies enough between mouse and human that the diligence read wants
  translation evidence.
- **Watch.** UM Ventures IP-status on PCT/US2025/037133 prosecution + national-phase
  filings; whether UM Ventures has an active licensing conversation with Merck KGaA
  on cardio-reperfusion or is positioning to shop the MoU to a competitor TLR7/8
  inhibitor program; Chao lab follow-on translational work (large-animal replication,
  additional TLR7/8 antagonist series validation); Center for Shock Trauma and
  Anesthesiology Research as a broader translational-anesthesiology sourcing pipeline
  beyond this one program.

### Walczak + Liang MIPOT multimodal imaging platform (UMB SoM Diagnostic Radiology cross JHU BME cross Kennedy Krieger F.M. Kirby Center) — landscape signal + licensable cross-institution imaging chassis ⚠
- **Lab / people.** **Yajie Liang, Ph.D.** — Assistant Professor, UMB SoM Department
  of Diagnostic Radiology and Nuclear Medicine; senior corresponding
  yajie.liang@som.umaryland.edu. **Piotr Walczak, M.D., Ph.D.** — Professor, UMB SoM
  Diagnostic Radiology + Nuclear Medicine; co-supervisor. **Miroslaw Janowski, M.D.,
  Ph.D.** — UMB Diagnostic Radiology co-author. Longstanding UMB translational-imaging
  bench, particularly CNS cell + gene therapy tracking. **Kexin Wang** (JHU
  Biomedical Engineering + Kennedy Krieger F.M. Kirby Center for Functional Brain
  Imaging) + **Jiadi Xu, Ph.D.** (Kirby Center + JHU School of Medicine Russell H.
  Morgan Department of Radiology and Radiological Science) provided the MRI-analysis
  contribution. **Tian-Ming Fu, Ph.D.** (Princeton ECE + Omenn Darling Bioengineering
  Institute) contributed to the multimodal viral labeling system. Cyclosporin A
  immunosuppression protocol for humanized-neuron xenografts.
- **Fresh disclosure (2026-07-14):** Wang J (first), Qiao G, Tan H, Russel C, Yang B,
  Li MJ, Wang K, Xu J, Chu C, Janowski M, Fu T-M, Walczak P (co-supervisor), Liang Y
  (senior corresponding), "Intravital Multimodal Imaging of Human Cortical Organoid
  Transplantation in a Mouse Model of Chronic Stroke," *Advanced Science* 2026,
  DOI 10.1002/advs.202515913, PMID 42447126, PMC13367743 (open access CC-BY).
  **COI verbatim:** "The authors declare no conflicts of interest." No patent, licensing,
  equity, or advisory disclosures anywhere in the extracted text. Funding block absent
  from the extracted PMC full text — needs re-check from publisher HTML if a
  funding-source diligence is needed.
- **Platform.** MIPOT (Multimodal Imaging Platform for Organoid Tracking) integrates
  four modalities on one animal: 9.4 T MRI (T2w + DTI/ADC/FA); bioluminescence imaging
  via Luc2-mCherry dual reporter driven by human ubiquitin C promoter; intraoperative
  surgical/light microscopy; intravital two-photon fluorescence microscopy using
  multicolor viral labels (cytosolic mCherry/TagBFP/iRFP-682 + nuclear H2B-GCaMP6s).
  Engineering novelty is a two-component 3D-printed removable head-fixation adapter
  (PLA on Bambu Lab X1-Carbon; STL files in supplement) that cycles the same animal
  through metal-free MRI + BLI + rigid-fixation TPFM sequentially.
- **Experimental design + honest read.** hiPSC (KOLF2.1J, male XY) cortical organoids
  at day 56 differentiation, quartered + injected into cleared photothrombotic
  Rose-Bengal stroke cavities in 3-4 mo male C57BL/6J mice at motor cortex, 10-day
  infarct maturation, cyclosporin A immunosuppression. BLI signal drops to ~50% at D4,
  ~25% at D7, ~17% at D10, ~5% at D14 (n=15). Endpoint histology at 2 + 4 weeks
  confirms limited human-graft persistence — STEM121+ / Ku80+ cells present but sparse
  TUJ1 co-labeling in cortex. **No behavioral/functional efficacy data.** Authors
  explicitly frame as tracking platform, not therapeutic candidate. Incidental
  hippocampal graft placements in 4/14 animals showed better neurite extension +
  TUJ1 — authors flag as descriptive only.
- **Blackbird relevance — two beats.** (1) **Licensable imaging chassis.** MIPOT is
  generalizable per the discussion to any organoid or CNS cell-therapy transplant
  experiment. Sits alongside Kalhor 3DEEP (§2b Kalhor lab spatial-omics) as cross-
  institution Baltimore imaging tools worth underwriting as licensable diligence
  infrastructure rather than as drug programs. Applies to Neuraly + Valted + Aletira
  orbit and any group developing CNS cell/organoid therapies. (2) **UM Ventures ×
  Blackbird ecosystem read.** This paper crosses UMB Diagnostic Radiology (Walczak +
  Liang + Janowski) with JHU BME (K. Wang) with Kennedy Krieger Kirby Center (Xu) in
  a single manuscript — exact operating pattern the UM Ventures × Blackbird
  co-investment surface is meant to seed. Walczak + Liang are the UMB translational-
  imaging bench Blackbird should already be tracking; this paper puts them on the map
  with a real cross-institution product. Reality-check read on CNS cell-therapy sector:
  the honest ~25% graft-persistence readout says every group graft-transplanting into
  infarct cavities needs longitudinal imaging like this to stop misreading their own
  experiments.
- **Watch.** UM Ventures IP-status on any MIPOT-associated method/apparatus filings
  (nothing disclosed in this paper; the 3D-printed head-fixation adapter is a specific
  engineering asset worth checking); Walczak + Liang commercial disposition (past
  academic-only track or open to spinout?); whether MIPOT gets pulled into a Neuraly
  or Aletira diligence-tool relationship as CNS cell/gene-therapy programs mature;
  Kalhor 3DEEP + MIPOT joint imaging-platform cluster underwriting as a Baltimore
  translational-imaging thesis.

### Gould lab UMB Neuropharmacology + Drug Discovery — EDP framework paper on the plastogen class + Perception Neuroscience royalty estate ⚠
- **Lab / people.** **Todd D. Gould, M.D.** — Professor, University of Maryland
  School of Medicine, Departments of Psychiatry, Pharmacology-Physiology and Drug
  Discovery, and Neurobiology; also Veterans Affairs Maryland Health Care System.
  **Co-inventor of (2R,6R)-hydroxynorketamine** — the active metabolite of ketamine
  that was licensed by **Perception Neuroscience** and subsequently rolled into
  **ATAI Life Sciences** via Perception acquisition. **UMB owns royalties on the
  (2R,6R)-HNK estate.** One of the more productive translational-neuropharmacology
  benches in academic psychiatry.
- **Fresh disclosure (2026-07-13):** Gould TD (first), Mathew SJ (Texas A&M College
  of Medicine + Baylor + VA), Fava M (MGH Psychiatry), Shekhar A (University of
  Pittsburgh SoM), "Enhancing plasticity to treat depression and other central
  nervous system diseases using event-driven pharmacology," *Journal of
  Psychopharmacology* 2026, DOI 10.1177/02698811261456206, PMID 42438931.
  Design-framework / opinion paper; co-authors are the depression + neuropsychiatric
  trialist establishment (Fava at MGH is the pre-eminent depression clinical trialist;
  Mathew ex-Baylor now Texas A&M co-authored the Perception HNK Phase 2 work;
  Shekhar is Pitt Med dean and long-time Indiana neuropsychiatric drug discovery).
- **Framework.** Proposes **event-driven pharmacology (EDP)** as design framework for
  the class of CNS drugs where a **transient binding event produces sustained changes
  in neuroplasticity that outlast drug exposure** — ketamine as paradigm case,
  classical psychedelics as extension, non-hallucinogenic neuroplastogens as
  design target. Distinct from **occupancy-driven pharmacology** (stable receptor
  occupancy over time, direct exposure-response). Argument: developing rapid-acting
  plastogens with the traditional occupancy-driven dose-optimization model is
  counterproductive — you optimize the dose for stable receptor occupancy and
  paradoxically get few durable benefits + more side effects, because you miss the
  actual underlying pharmacology. New biomarker + clinical-dose-optimization methods
  needed for EDP-class assets.
- **Blackbird relevance — landscape signal, not a sourcing lead.** (1) UMB
  Neuropharmacology + Drug Discovery thought-leadership positioning on how to design
  + evaluate the plastogen class shapes diligence conversations on every ketamine-
  adjacent and psychedelic-adjacent asset the venture community sees for the next
  two years — the Bavarian-inference bounds a translational investor uses when reading
  a plastogen pitch will start invoking EDP framing. (2) **Design-space read for the
  Lieber neuropsych NewCo second-and-third-asset conversation.** The GPR52-agonist
  lead is by design chronic-agonist occupancy-driven (three-symptom-domain thesis)
  — correct for the mechanism, not a mistake. But the pipeline expansion question is
  whether the neuropsych NewCo's second + third shots should sit in the plastogen/EDP
  class rather than the chronic-agonist class. Design-space input for Hemaka + Third
  Rock conversation on NewCo pipeline shape, not a fresh sourcing lead.
- **Watch.** Whether Gould has additional in-prosecution IP outside the (2R,6R)-HNK
  license to Perception Neuroscience — his current work on translationally-tractable
  neuroplastogens is worth tracking as a UMB pipeline signal; whether the UMB Drug
  Discovery Program is positioning any next-generation neuroplastogen chemotype for
  a new licensing conversation; Perception Neuroscience Phase 2 readouts in TRD as
  the natural (2R,6R)-HNK clinical calendar for anyone paying attention.

### Cao lab endplate-osteoclast netrin-1/DCC LBP amplification loop (JHU School of Medicine Department of Orthopedic Surgery + Department of Biomedical Engineering and Institute of Cell Engineering) — active sourcing lead + JHTV provisional-conversation moment ⚠
- **Lab / people.** **Xu Cao, Ph.D.** — Lee Riley Professor of Orthopaedic Surgery, JHU School of Medicine; joint appointments in Department of Orthopedic Surgery + Department of Biomedical Engineering and Institute of Cell Engineering; senior corresponding xcao11@jhmi.edu. Fifteen-year publication + IP track record on the skeleton-as-signaling-organ axis (TGF-β1 in aged bone, osteoclast-secreted factors in bone-marrow adiposity, endplate biology in chronic pain). **Dayu Pan** (first; JHU Ortho + BME/ICE) drives the netrin-1/DCC axis characterization. **Mei Wan** (senior co-author; JHU Ortho + BME/ICE) — separate but complementary bone-cerebrovascular-axis lab (2026-07-15 *J Clin Invest* review PMID 42454488 sets that framework). External collaborators: **James C. Iatridis** + Philip Nasser + Damien M Laudier (Mount Sinai Orthopedics), **Zhaozhu Qiu** (JHU Physiology), **Bradley K. Taylor** (Pittsburgh Center for Pain Research + Pittsburgh Project to end Opioid Misuse).
- **Fresh disclosure (2026-07-14):** Pan D (first), Shen M, Abatan E, Zheng J, Nasser P, Laudier DM, Kong C, Yan L, Qiu Z, Iatridis JC, Wan M, Zheng J, Taylor BK, Cao X (senior corresponding), "Dorsal horn DCC amplification loop induced by endplate osteoclasts generates chronic nociplastic low back pain in male mice," *Nature Communications* 2026-07-14, DOI 10.1038/s41467-026-75423-9, PMID 42448693. Open access CC BY-NC-ND. **COI verbatim:** "The authors declare no competing interests." Funding exclusively NIH National Institute on Aging (P01AG066603, R01AG076783, R01AG068997 — all to X.C.).
- **Mechanism — end-to-end amplification loop with druggable nodes at each step.** Spine degeneration → porous endplates → **Trap+ osteoclasts secrete netrin-1** (classical axon-guidance ligand repurposed to peripheral bone-pain biology in Cao's recent work) → **netrin-1 activates DCC on Advillin+ (Avil+) DRG sensory neurons** innervating the endplate → **DCC signaling triggers Src-kinase phosphorylation + SNARE-complex docking in presynaptic terminals** → sensory-neuron terminals **ramp up glutamate release into the dorsal horn** → **DH neurons upregulate both netrin-1 and DCC themselves** as postsynaptic plasticity, closing a self-amplifying positive feedback loop → **axis extends up into parabrachial nucleus** (ascending pain-affect circuit) in both LBP and naturally-aged mice.
- **Genetic validation.** Conditional netrin-1 KO in Trap+ osteoclasts reduces spinal hypersensitivity (osteoclast-restricted deletion — first genetic leg is causal). Conditional Dcc KO in Avil+ DRG neurons reduces spinal hypersensitivity (sensory-neuron-restricted deletion — second genetic leg is causal). Effect replicates in **naturally-aged mice** (not lesion-model artifact — biology is showing up in the age-related LBP phenotype the actual patient population lives with).
- **Sourcing significance — strongest JHU direct sourcing lead this month.** (1) **Unmet need.** Non-specific chronic low back pain is world's leading cause of years lived with disability, no mechanism-defined therapy, one-in-six-adults patient population; two decades of failed programs (nerve-growth-factor antibodies, TRPV1 antagonists, cathepsin-K inhibitors — all flunked at human-efficacy step). (2) **Two clean therapeutic intervention points fall out.** (a) **Netrin-1 or DCC-directed biologic** — neutralizing antibody, aptamer, or soluble decoy at endplate-DRG interface; NetrisPharma (Lyon) has anti-netrin-1 antibody NP137 already in oncology trials so the therapeutic-antibody path against netrin-1 is precedented (but not in the pain indication). Netrin-1 is small enough that a peptide-mimetic or fragment-based small-molecule approach is not out of the question. (b) **Osteoclast-endplate-targeted small molecule** aimed at shutting down netrin-1 output at its source — sits inside bone-therapeutics chemistry space where Cao lab + JHU Ortho has 15-year IP track record + natural fit for what a translational venture office knows how to build. (3) **IP fact pattern.** COI-clean, single-institution JHU + NIH-funded — "no licenses yet, NIH grants paying for it, single-institution" pattern Blackbird wants for a JHTV provisional-conversation moment.
- **Diligence questions.** (1) **Sex-specificity.** Paper is male-mice-only (title states "in male mice"). Post-Huganir 2026-07-07 PSD-turnover findings on sex-differentiated synaptic-protein stability, sex-stratified pain phenotyping is now industry-standard bar. Female-mouse replication is next-cycle question before program is under-writable. (2) **Human tissue validation.** Every non-specific-LBP failure over two decades flunked at human step. Human tissue + post-mortem validation of the netrin-1/DCC axis in patient endplates + DRG is next-cycle question a Series-A committee will ask. (3) **Competitive intelligence.** Anti-netrin-1 antibody path (NetrisPharma NP137) is oncology-precedented but not in the pain indication. DCC-targeted or osteoclast-endplate-targeted path in pain remains white space. Blackbird sits closer to that white space than any commercial entrant does.
- **Action items.** (a) **JHTV conversation this week (Eddie + Hemaka).** Is a provisional filed? Field-of-use structure (composition-of-matter vs methods-of-use, LBP vs peripheral pain vs bone-marrow adiposity)? If no filing in prosecution, this is a shape-of-a-company moment — Cao is exactly the senior operator caliber to carry a company. If filing in prosecution, licensing terms + founder disposition are the two conversations to have first. (b) Pair with **Wan lab bone-cerebrovascular-axis JCI review (2026-07-15 PMID 42454488)** — signals that JHU Ortho is systematically claiming the "skeleton-as-endocrine-organ" IP landscape and Wan is a natural next-cycle sourcing target.
- **Watch.** JHTV posture on the netrin-1/DCC endplate-DRG amplification loop IP (composition-of-matter on biologics, methods-of-use on osteoclast-endplate small molecules, field-of-use structure); Cao lab follow-on female-mouse replication + human tissue validation; NetrisPharma NP137 clinical readouts in oncology as prior-art anchor for the anti-netrin-1 biologic path; Mei Wan lab (JHU Ortho + BME/ICE) as second Cao-adjacent sourcing lead as bone-cerebrovascular-axis primary paper follows the 2026-07-15 review; whether Iatridis Mount Sinai Ortho side of the collaboration is IP-encumbered or purely academic.

### Mao lab JHU INBT anionic LNP cleavable-crosslinker platform (JHU Institute for NanoBioTechnology + BME + Materials Science + Translational Therapeutic and Regenerative Engineering Center) — active sourcing lead + platform IP for splenic T-cell mRNA delivery ⚠
- **Lab / people.** **Hai-Quan Mao, Ph.D.** — Professor of Materials Science and Engineering + BME + JHU School of Medicine Departments of Biomedical Engineering; Vice Provost for Research at Johns Hopkins; senior corresponding hmao@jhu.edu. Also Director of the **Institute for NanoBioTechnology (INBT)** and the **Translational Therapeutic and Regenerative Engineering Center**. One of JHU's more prolific translational-materials benches — serial nanotechnology commercialization history through INBT. **Yunhe Su** (first author; JHU INBT + BME + Translational Therapeutic and Regenerative Engineering Center) — driving the anionic-LNP crosslinker chemistry.
- **Fresh disclosure (2026-07-16):** Su Y (first), Choy J, Liu X, Zhu Y, Lin J, Wei C, Goodier KD, Yu D, Cheng L, Patel M, Lu X, Ma J, Wang J, Mao HQ (senior corresponding), "Stabilizing Anionic mRNA Lipid Nanoparticles by Cleavable Crosslinking of Cholesterol," *ACS Applied Materials & Interfaces* 2026-07-16, DOI 10.1021/acsami.6c06888, PMID 42461776. Hopkins-only manuscript (all authors at INBT + BME + Materials Science + Translational Therapeutic and Regenerative Engineering Center). No COI or patent-application disclosure surfaced through r.jina.ai + PubMed abstract text.
- **Platform — the actual product problem this chemistry closes.** Anionic helper lipids can bias LNP expression away from the liver (the default LNP hepatic-tropism problem) and toward the spleen — the tissue you want if your payload is a tolerogenic mRNA for autoimmunity, an in-vivo mRNA-CAR-T construct, or a T-cell-directed cancer vaccine. But anionic helpers weaken the RNA-lipid interaction, so nanoparticles fall apart during purification. Every anionic-LNP program hits this ceiling. Mao's chemistry adds a bolt-on **acid-cleavable cholesterol crosslinker + PEG-diamine** that stabilizes the particle **without swapping the primary lipid constituents of the parent formulation** — you keep whatever ionizable-lipid work your program has invested in and just add the cholesterol tether. Acid-cleavable means the tether releases in the endosome, so payload release is preserved. **Crosslinker length is tunable** — chemistry landscape for follow-on medicinal-chemistry programs.
- **Results.** Optimized crosslinked formulation delivers **significant increase in splenic mRNA expression at 12 h** vs uncrosslinked control. **33.2% of splenic CD45+ tdTomato+ cells identified by flow are T cells** — payload is not just landing in the spleen, it is transducing lymphocytes. That is the exact result an in-vivo cell-therapy program is trying to buy. Mechanistic analyses point to controlled mRNA release + altered intracellular processing.
- **Sourcing significance — direct sourcing lead + platform IP.** Platform IP wraps every downstream therapeutic program touching the anionic corner of LNP design space. Three separate multi-billion-dollar therapeutic areas fall out: (1) **Tolerogenic mRNA for autoimmunity** — Cabaletta + Vor Bio + Kernel active competitive space; splenic-biased delivery is the workhorse formulation for tolerance induction. (2) **In-vivo mRNA-CAR-T** — Umoja Biopharma + Capstan Therapeutics + Orbital Therapeutics + Cartesian Therapeutics + Moderna are racing toward this as commercial CAR-T economics wobble; in-vivo formulation skips the ex-vivo T-cell engineering step. (3) **T-cell-directed cancer vaccines** targeted at splenic marginal-zone antigen presentation. Bolt-on to any existing anionic-LNP program a Blackbird portfolio-adjacent NewCo might be building. **Direct portfolio adjacency to Luetkens UMB cathepsin-B-CAR-T story** (§2b) — an in-vivo mRNA-CAR-T formulation chassis pairs with an adjunct that preserves CAR-T persistence for a two-asset NewCo thesis.
- **Diligence questions.** (1) **IP status.** Is the cleavable-cholesterol crosslinker composition-of-matter filed at JHTV, and is the tunable-length claim inside that filing or a separate application? Manuscript itself does not disclose a filing. (2) **Extension breadth.** Whether the crosslinker chemistry generalizes to other anionic helper lipids (DOTAP variants, phosphatidylserine derivatives, non-cholesterol anchors) or is limited to the cholesterol-anchor formulation demonstrated here. (3) **Mao founder disposition.** Mao has serial INBT-anchored commercial history — does he carve this out as its own platform vehicle, add it to an existing partnership, or license out non-exclusively? Different investment thesis in each case.
- **Watch.** JHTV posture on the crosslinker composition-of-matter + tunable-length medicinal-chemistry claim; Mao founder disposition; anionic-LNP splenic-biased mRNA competitive landscape (Nutcracker Therapeutics, Anjarium Biosciences, Orbital Therapeutics, Cartesian Therapeutics, Umoja Biopharma, Capstan Therapeutics); pairing conversation with Luetkens cathepsin-B-CAR-T UMB program as a candidate two-asset NewCo thesis on in-vivo mRNA-CAR-T + persistence-preserving adjunct.

### Fried lab JHU Chemistry looped-translation ultra-large-fibrous-protein biomanufacturing platform — active sourcing lead + declared JHTV patent filing + second fully-incubated BioHub NewCo candidate model ⚠
- **Lab / people.** **Stephen D. Fried, Ph.D.** — Assistant Professor / senior faculty, Department of Chemistry, Johns Hopkins University; senior corresponding sdfried@jhu.edu. Prior program on translation-mechanism biology + chaperone-directed protein engineering; not a one-off — the lab has been publishing steadily toward this endpoint. **Qi Xie, Ph.D.** (first author) — driving the circular-RNA looped-translation chemistry + inventor on the patent filing. **Louis J. Papa III + Anton M. Barybin + Michael Xiong** (co-authors; JHU Chemistry). **Matthew D. Shoulders, Ph.D.** (MIT Chemistry) — outside collaborator on transcript-engineering side; co-inventor carve-out on the MIT side of the joint IP.
- **Fresh disclosure (2026-07-17):** Xie Q (first), Papa LJ III, Barybin AM, Xiong M, Shoulders MD, Fried SD (senior corresponding), "Synthesis of Ultra-Large Fibrous Proteins from Bacteria via a Looped-Translation System," *bioRxiv* v1 2026.07.16.738376, posted 2026-07-17. **COI verbatim:** "QX and SDF are filing for a patent related to techniques described in this work." **Active JHTV patent filing declared in manuscript** — filing predates the preprint (JHTV had eyes on this pre-preprint). Funding: NIGMS R35-GM161721 (Fried), NSF CAREER MCB-2045844, Sloan Foundation, Dreyfus Foundation, NIGMS R35-GM136354 (Shoulders MIT), NSF Graduate Fellowship. CC-BY-NC-ND 4.0.
- **Platform — the specific engineering.** Circular-RNA-based ribosome translation system: linear mRNA re-engineered into a covalently closed circular transcript, ribosome walks the loop iteratively concatenating polypeptide until an exit signal terminates. **Three engineering pieces.** (1) **Synonymous-codon locker sequences** — codon-optimization trick suppresses folding-and-stalling problem that repetitive tracts otherwise create during translation. (2) **RNA-circularization chaperones** promote efficient loop formation across repetitive transcripts (identified specific chaperones that work). (3) **Ribosome-traffic mathematical model** identifies translation bottlenecks around the circular transcript, letting the engineering team place stalling motifs where they need to be — turns platform optimization into a computable problem. **Secretion coupled via programmed ribosomal frameshift as exit signal** — polypeptide released + exported across membrane in both E. coli AND food-grade Bacillus subtilis (industrial-scale fermentation host with existing regulatory + manufacturing infrastructure).
- **Results.** Generalizes across ≥6 classes of fibrous proteins: spidroin silk, tropoelastin elastin, multiple collagen family members. Products exceed titin (largest known natural polypeptide) at **3.8 megadaltons** — this platform manufactures polypeptides larger than any known natural protein. Material-science characterization is real: electron microscopy on products, bulk-material fabrication samples, mechanical analysis showing tensile strength + resilience + elasticity profile associated with ultra-high-MW polypeptides. Two-decade unsolved problem in recombinant production of fibrous proteins — Bolt Threads + Spiber (industrial commercial competitors on silk-alike production) have never reached this MW.
- **Sourcing significance — highest-value direct sourcing lead of the weekend.** Blackbird-audience-legible commercial addressable space is broader than the abstract's "bioplastics + engineered living materials" framing lets on. **Three biomedical applications sit inside immediate reach.** (1) **Recombinant collagen matrices** — the wound-care, surgical-mesh, and tissue-engineering-scaffold markets currently filled by Humacyte + Organogenesis + Integra Life Sciences with donor-tissue or animal-sourced collagen. A recombinant human-sequence collagen with tunable MW + native mechanical properties is what every regenerative-medicine + reconstructive-surgery program has been asking for. (2) **Recombinant elastin** for vascular grafts + dermal fillers + elastomeric drug-delivery matrices. **Elastagen exit comp:** sold to Allergan 2018 for elastin platform at valuation >$100M without reaching ultra-high MW. (3) **Spider-silk sutures + surgical adhesives** — defensible medical-materials space that Bolt Threads + Spiber never entered because their platforms could not reach required MW ranges. Single-institution JHU IP with MIT co-inventor carve-out on Shoulders side — standard JHTV-led negotiation with MIT co-inventorship carve-out template.
- **IP posture.** **Active filing declared in manuscript**, filing predates preprint = JHTV had visibility on this well before publication. Not a provisional filed and forgotten — an ongoing filing at the moment of preprint posting. That is the diligence-legible IP story a translational venture office wants to see.
- **Actions.** (a) **Eddie — JHTV patent-status conversation** on field-of-use structure (composition-of-matter vs method claims separation?), active licensing conversation status with strategic partners (Modern Meadow, Ginkgo, industrial-fermentation majors). If no active licensing conversation, this is a founding-conversation moment with Fried. (b) **Avi + Hemaka — second fully-incubated BioHub NewCo candidate model** alongside Aletira. A Blackbird-incubated bio-manufacturing platform based on a JHTV-licensed translation system with pre-clinical proof-of-concept on collagen + elastin + silk simultaneously would demonstrate the Blackbird flagship model works twice. (c) **Diligence questions.** Process-scale titer at 10L + 100L fermenter (biology works; industrial yield is next-cycle question). Sequence-verified MW distribution + batch consistency (repetitive polypeptides are hard to sequence; matters for regulatory + materials-manufacturing conversations). Endotoxin + host-protein contamination profile on medical-materials side (Bacillus subtilis preferred; platform works in Bacillus, so answer probably exists). Fried founder disposition — young senior faculty in translational-materials-friendly department; manuscript reads as a group building toward a defined commercial endpoint.
- **Watch.** JHTV patent-filing prosecution status + field-of-use structure; Fried lab follow-on preprints extending the platform to therapeutic-relevant target proteins (functional proteins, not just structural fibrous ones); Shoulders MIT-side IP posture on the transcript-engineering-chaperone chemistry (does MIT co-inventor carve-out create a joint-venture licensing dynamic or does JHTV run the license); competitive biomanufacturing spinouts + strategic partnering conversations (Ginkgo, Modern Meadow, Colorifix, Bolt Threads, Spiber).

### Deredge (UMB SoP) + Freel Meyers (JHU SoM Pharmacology) MInt-HDX cross-institution ML/HDX-MS docking platform — active sourcing lead + open JHTV+UM-Ventures joint provisional-filing surface + cross-institution UM Ventures × Blackbird pattern paper ⚠
- **Lab / people.** **Daniel Deredge, Ph.D.** — senior corresponding, UMB School of Pharmacy, Department of Pharmaceutical Sciences; dderedge@rx.umaryland.edu. HDX-mass-spec-plus-computational bench; ships tools drug-discovery pipelines want. **Caren L. Freel Meyers, Ph.D.** — co-senior, JHU School of Medicine, Department of Pharmacology & Molecular Sciences. Established antibiotic + metabolic-enzyme medicinal chemist. **Vincent Lowe** (first author; Deredge lab). Ally K. Smith, Rinky Parakra, Eucolona Toci (co-authors).
- **Fresh disclosure (2026-07-16):** Lowe V (first), Smith AK, Parakra R, Toci E, Freel Meyers CL (co-senior; JHU SoM Pharmacology & Molecular Sciences), Deredge D (senior corresponding; UMB SoP Pharmaceutical Sciences), "MInt-HDX: Leveraging Hydrogen-Deuterium Exchange Mass Spectrometry and Machine-Learning to Improve Protein-Ligand Docking," *bioRxiv* v1 2026.07.14.738285, posted 2026-07-16. **COI verbatim:** "The authors have declared no competing interest." Funding: NIH T32 GM158458 + R01GM143810 (Deredge).
- **Platform.** Hybrid physics-plus-machine-learning framework for protein-ligand docking. **XGBoost gradient-boosted-decision-tree model** trained on differential HDX-MS signatures across 11 protein-ligand systems (1,032 individual peptides). Model predicts, for a new protein-ligand system with its own HDX-MS readout, which residues are actually contacting the ligand. **Two-step docking workflow.** (1) HDX-guided candidate docking sites generated by feeding predicted-interacting-residue set into a 3D geometric clustering + convex-hull step — the model tells the physics-based docking engine (GLIDE, AutoDock, Rosetta) where to look. (2) After physics-based docking generates candidate poses, the same XGBoost model filters + re-scores poses based on HDX-MS consistency. **Validation.** Three protein-ligand systems benchmarked — Ligand-RMSD <3 Å from crystallographic ligand conformation using only solution-phase HDX-MS data + no co-crystal structure required. Competitive with best pure-physics or pure-ML docking approaches.
- **Sourcing significance — direct sourcing lead + cross-institution pattern paper.** **Second cross-institution JHU + UMB computational-tools paper in less than a month**, alongside Popel-Fertig-Deshpande spatial-QSP PNAS 2026-07-14 (§2b Popel-Green-Pandey cluster). That is a **pattern signal** — the cross-institution computational-oncology + computational-drug-discovery axis is where the UM Ventures × Blackbird co-investment surface should be spending time. Broad customer base — every major pharma HDX-MS core (J&J, Pfizer, Merck, Roche, Novartis, AstraZeneca) + every serious structure-based-discovery biotech (Structure Therapeutics, Relay Therapeutics, Schrödinger, Nimbus Therapeutics). Value proposition: collapses HDX-MS run → diligence-legible pose model from weeks of manual interpretation to automated processing. Licensable software platform with installed-base-driven revenue model.
- **IP posture.** **COI declared as none** = JHTV + UM Ventures joint provisional-filing window open. Federal funding (NIH T32 + R01 to Deredge). Composition-of-workflow claims still patentable even if training code is released open-source with the paper (open-methods-plus-closed-workflow-composition is a common academic strategy). Cross-institution JHU + UMB co-corresponding structure — exact framework the UM Ventures × Blackbird BioVentures co-investment agreement was set up to support (aSKY-track precedent).
- **Actions.** (a) **Yixuan + Avi — JHTV + UM Ventures joint provisional-filing status check** on MInt-HDX composition-of-workflow claims this week; open-methods-plus-closed-workflow-composition IP posture check. (b) **Deredge + Freel Meyers founder-disposition read** — commercial-vehicle conversation or academic-methods-only contribution? Answer shapes founding-conversation vs licensing-conversation. (c) **Esther** — add Deredge (UMB SoP Pharmaceutical Sciences) to tracked-lab list; pull Deredge patent history off Justia; pull Freel Meyers JHTV footprint separately (established Hopkins medicinal chemist, likely has patent estate).
- **Watch.** JHTV + UM Ventures joint provisional-filing status; Deredge lab follow-on preprints extending the framework beyond docking (HDX-MS-guided allosteric-site identification, HDX-MS-guided cryptic-pocket prediction); whether MInt-HDX code release is accompanied by any patent-application filings that surface later; strategic-partner licensing conversations (Structure Therapeutics + Relay + Schrödinger + Nimbus + big-pharma HDX-MS cores as natural counterparties); Freel Meyers-side JHU-only IP footprint for potential companion licensing bundles.

### Kornberg lab LXR-driven remyelination in MS (JHU Neurology; Calabresi co-author) — active sourcing lead + 2026-08-16 Radar LEAD ⚠
- **Lab / people.** **Michael D. Kornberg, M.D., Ph.D.** — JHU School of Medicine, Department of Neurology; senior/corresponding. **Peter A. Calabresi, M.D.** (JHU Neurology; one of the top MS clinician-scientists in the US) on the author list = fundable pedigree + clinical-translation channel. First author Matthew D. Smith; collaborators incl. Norman J. Haughey (JHU lipidomics), Jack P. Antel / Qiao-Ling Cui (Montreal Neurological Institute, human OL-lineage cells), Xitiz Chamling (hPSC-OPC reporter line).
- **Fresh disclosure (2026-08-13):** Smith MD, Deng X, Hu J, …, Haughey NJ, Calabresi PA, Kornberg MD (senior), "Remodeling oligodendrocyte lipid metabolism via liver X receptors overcomes inflammatory blockade of remyelination," bioRxiv v1 posted 2026-08-13 (DOI 10.64898/2026.08.07.743529). Full text read (144KB via r.jina.ai on v1.full). **COI VERBATIM: "MDK has received consulting fees from Genentech, TG Therapeutics, and OptumRx on topics unrelated to this manuscript. Other authors declare that they have no competing interests."** = NO equity, NO company, NO patent on this biology. Funding all society/foundation (National MS Society, Race to Erase MS, Wieden Family Foundation, JHU Catalyst Award, Karen Toffler Charitable Trust, Torrey MS Endowment, Maryland Stem Cell Research Fund, Gilbert Family Foundation).
- **Mechanism.** IFN-γ (saturates MS lesions) reprograms OPC lipid metabolism from synthesis to fatty-acid oxidation → depletes free fatty acids OPCs need to build myelin membrane → blocks maturation into myelinating oligodendrocytes + drives the aberrant immune-like antigen-presenting "iOLG" phenotype. Signature confirmed in mouse OPCs, human surgical OL-lineage cells, and human MS-lesion snRNA-seq. Restoring lipids (oleic acid supplementation; DGAT1 inhibition; FAO block) reverses it. **LXR (liver X receptor) = the upstream master switch** — pharmacologic activation (tool agonists GW3965 + structurally distinct T0901317) rebalances metabolism, suppresses iOLG immune functions (dose-dependent; abolished in LXRα/β double-KO), and overcomes the IFN-γ differentiation block in mouse AND human hPSC-derived OPCs (MBP-luciferase). In vivo (adoptive-transfer-cuprizone, inflammation directly blocks repair): GW3965 10 mg/kg/day IP started day-3 post-transfer (after immune infiltration, to isolate the repair-side effect — confirmed minimal change to CNS immune infiltrate) increased mature oligodendrocytes (Olig2+ASPA+) + myelin (fluoromyelin).
- **Sourcing significance — direct lead, clears the "investor takes the meeting" bar well.** Remyelination = marquee validated whitespace in MS (all approved DMTs are immunomodulatory, none repairs myelin; prior remyelination bets — Biogen anti-LINGO/opicinumab, clemastine — could nudge differentiation under clean conditions but NOT overcome the inflammatory brake, the whole game in a real patient). Fresh mechanism + human validation + in-vivo repair + CLEAN unencumbered JHTV license path + founding-caliber team.
- **CANDID CATCH / the build.** Mechanism/target-validation paper, NOT a molecule (tool compounds only). LXR has a KNOWN liability: broad LXRα activation drives hepatic steatosis + hypertriglyceridemia (stalled prior LXR programs). The company = "build or in-license a brain-penetrant, liver-sparing LXR modulator against a newly validated rationale"; paper notes **LXRβ** is the isotype expressed in OPCs = natural handle for a selective/liver-sparing molecule. That selectivity is both the central risk AND the moat.
- **Actions.** Avi — is a β-selective, CNS-penetrant LXR modulator tractable medchem + who already has chemical matter to in-license around (LXRβ-biased modulators, prior-art landscape). Eddie — open the JHTV field-of-use conversation now (provisional-filing moment; Kornberg + Calabresi is founding-team caliber).
- **Watch.** JHTV provisional-filing status on the LXR-remyelination-in-MS use/mechanism; Kornberg + Calabresi founder disposition; competitive remyelination programs (anti-LINGO estate, clemastine follow-ons, other metabolic-reprogramming approaches); any commercial LXRβ-selective/liver-sparing chemistry programs to in-license.

### Doetzlhofer lab ZBTB20 / midkine–PTPRZ1 cochlear supporting-cell regeneration (JHU SOM Solomon H. Snyder Neuroscience + Otolaryngology–HNS / Center for Hearing and Balance) — active sourcing lead + 2026-08-23 Radar LEAD + direct Aletira payload adjacency ⚠
- **Lab / people:** **Angelika Doetzlhofer, Ph.D.** (senior). First author **Charles T. Morgan** (NIDCD F31 fellow); **Zia Ur Rehman** (now UTMB Otolaryngology). Zbtb20-floxed mice from **Ulrich Mueller (JHU SOM)**. Lab's prior line of work: **LIN28B / TRIM71 / HMGA2** reprogramming of cochlear supporting cells.
- **Paper:** "Loss of Zbtb20 disrupts cochlear supporting cell differentiation and maturation and extends the postnatal hair cell regenerative window in mice," bioRxiv **10.64898/2026.08.19.745776v1**, posted **2026-08-20**. Full text obtained via `r.jina.ai` on the **`v1.full.pdf`** (the `.full` HTML render was abstract-only).
- **Core claim:** **ZBTB20 is the maintained transcriptional brake that closes the postnatal hair-cell regenerative window.** Emx2-Cre KO delays supporting-cell cell-cycle exit, differentiation and maturation and leaves progenitor genes (**Hmga2, Sox11**) switched on at P5 (676 DEGs, 489 up / 187 down, q<0.05). Purified **Lfng-GFP+** KO supporting cells form organoids at **~3× the control rate**, larger, more proliferative, and yield significantly more MYO7A+/SOX2+ hair cells per organoid; **Pou4f3-DTR** explants show more hair cells in damaged *and* undamaged tissue.
- **The result that makes it a target rather than a developmental story:** **ACUTE, doxycycline-inducible deletion in already-formed postnatal tissue still increases organoid size and formation rate** → the repression is *maintained*, not a one-time developmental setting. Acute-KO RNA-seq (524 DEGs) identifies **Ptprz1** upregulation; **exogenous midkine (MDK) at 10/50/100 ng/mL reproduces the phenotype** (size up at all doses, formation rate up at 10 and 50 but **not** 100 — a real ceiling). **The transcription factor is undruggable; the secreted ligand and cell-surface receptor beneath it are not.**
- **IP posture — CLEAN, the Blackbird sweet spot.** "The authors declare no competing interests." Funding **NIDCD R01DC019359 + F31DC020882** and the **David M. Rubenstein Fund for Hearing Research**. No pharma sponsor, no existing company, no equity → open JHTV license path. Data: GEO **GSE342694**.
- **Competitive precedent to state up front: Frequency Therapeutics.** Same thesis (wake Lgr5+ cochlear supporting cells with small molecules), **FX-322 missed speech perception and every secondary in a 142-patient Phase 2b (Feb 2023)**; company discontinued FX-322 and FX-345, cut **~55%** of staff, pivoted to remyelination in MS, then reverse-merged into Korro Bio. **Differentiator to lead with: Frequency pushed generic stemness pathways (Wnt/HDAC) without knowing what enforced the mature state — this work names the enforcer and supplies a specific downstream ligand–receptor axis. Frame as "the field pushed the wrong node," never as "hair-cell regeneration, take four."**
- **ALETIRA ADJACENCY — the reason this is ours.** MDK–PTPRZ1 is a proliferation axis with **oncology history (PTPRZ1 is a glioma target)**; you cannot broadcast it into the ear. A therapy needs expression **restricted to supporting cells, at a controlled level, in a small semi-enclosed compartment** — SELEXON's exact premise, and supporting cells are already the target cell type for the GJB2 programs (Skylark, Sensorion). **Framing for Avi + Hemaka: evaluate as a SECOND PAYLOAD for SELEXON delivered locally into the cochlea, where expression restriction is the safety story, not only as a standalone NewCo.**
- **Candid limits:** organoid + explant only; no in vivo regeneration in a mature animal; **no ABR / functional hearing recovery**; all P5 mouse tissue; acute deletion produced hair cells only in **damaged** explants.
- **DILIGENCE ASK (one experiment, and it is the whole investment case): does MDK–PTPRZ1 activation raise hair-cell counts in an ADULT mouse cochlea in vivo after noise or ototoxic damage, with an ABR readout?**
- **Actions:** **Esther** — open the conversation this week. **Eddie** — provisional-filing moment on the **MDK–PTPRZ1 axis specifically**, not on ZBTB20. **Avi** — is there existing chemical matter (MDK mimetics, PTPRZ1 agonists/antagonists) to in-license around, and who holds it?

### Elisseeff lab fibroid-senescence atlas (JHU) — WATCH, not a build (deferred 08-09 lead, closed 2026-08-16) ⚠
- **Lab / people.** **Jennifer H. Elisseeff, Ph.D.** (JHU; senior) with Mostafa Borahay + James Segars (JHU Gyn/Ob) and **Elana Fertig** (UMB genome sciences). "Senescent cell networks link matrix remodeling and vascular dysfunction in human fibroids," bioRxiv v1 2026-08-07 (DOI 10.64898/2026.08.06.743362) — full text NOW RENDERED (157KB) and read 2026-08-16.
- **Science.** First single-cell "senotype" map of human uterine fibroids: senescent cells concentrate in mural (~61%), fibroblast, and a fibroid-expanded endothelial compartment; drive ECM remodeling (collagen VI / COL6A3), impaired vessel maturation, and immune signaling via TEAD4/YAP mechanosensing programs. Translational hook: in tissue from a JHU Phase I injectable-collagenase fibroid trial (Singh/Borahay), collagenase reduced both ECM density + p16+ senescent burden.
- **Read — WATCH not build.** Huge underserved market (fibroids affect 70-80% of women; leading cause of hysterectomy; little non-surgical) BUT (1) IP thin/derivative — descriptive atlas; the actionable therapy (collagenase) is already clinical + commoditized (Endo's collagenase clostridium histolyticum); the novel TEAD4/COL6A3 mechanosensing driver is a hypothesis, not a validated target; (2) **COI HEAVY (encumbrance flag): J.H.E. holds equity in Unity Biotechnology + Aegeria Soft Tissue, consultant for Tessara; E.J.F. SAB/consulting incl. familial relationship to a founder of PushCART Therapeutics** — serial senescence/ECM founder, so any fibroid-senescence IP likely reads onto existing vehicles. Keep on watch list; not a clean new company.

## 3. How to use this in the show

- **Sourcing Radar (daily):** a JHU/UMB/Lieber paper is a strong lead if it has
  a novel target, a platform, or a translational result with defensible IP and
  unmet need — i.e. the kind of thing Blackbird would license and incubate.
  Bonus if it sits adjacent to a portfolio thesis above (selective gene
  delivery, neuropsych GPCRs, gut-restricted small molecules, RNA upregulation,
  IO).
- **Portfolio Watch (weekly):** anything moving the competitive/regulatory/
  financing picture for the programs above. Always name the program it touches
  and call the implication (tailwind / threat / validation / watch-item).
